      \        During Civil Construction\\n           Contractor\\n            Additional Requirements     Adhoc\

      \      The Contractor shall prepare an Event Contingency Plan under his Site Environmental Plan. The\\n  \

  text: Impact monitoring at air monitoring stations must be carried out for two continuous twenty-four-hour days
    every fifteen days and where perceived air quality problems exist.

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 75,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n259\\\
      n\\n          47.3.13  The Contractor shall design and implement his blasting techniques so as to minimise\\\
      n                     dust, noise, vibration generation and prevention fly rock.\\n\\n\\n          47.3.14\
      \   Blasting technique should be consistent not only with nature and quaintly of rock to be\\n           \
      \         blasted but also the location of blasting.\\n\\n          47.3.15  The  contractor  shall  give\
      \  preference  to  explosives  with  better  environmental\\n                      characteristics.\\n\\n\
      \          47.3.16  The Contractor shall protect structures, utilities, pavements roads and other facilities\\\
      n                  from disfiguration and damage as a result of his activities. Where this is not possible,\\\
      n                    the contractor  shall restore the structures,  utilities, pavements, roads and other\\\
      n                          facilities to their original or better, failing which the rectification/restoration\
      \ work shall\\n                be carried out at the risk and cost of the contractor.\\n\\n\\n          47.3.17\
      \  The Contractor shall submit to the Employer an Air Monitoring and Control Plan\\n               (AMCP)\
      \ under contract specific Site Environmental Plan to guide construction activity\\n                     insofar\
      \ as it relates to monitoring, controlling and mitigating air pollution.\\n\\n\\n          47.4    AIR MONITORING\\\
      n          47.4.1    Construction activities that will generate dust impacts include excavation (including\\\
      n                     related activities), material handling and stockpiling, vehicular movement, and wind\\\
      n                   erosion of unpaved work areas.\\n\\n          47.4.2 The impact of fugitive dust on ambient\
      \ air pollution depends on the quantity generated, as\\n                     well as the drift potential of\
      \ the dust particles injected into the atmosphere. Large dust\\n                      particles will settle\
      \ out near the source and smaller particles are likely to undergo\\n                    dispersal over greater\
      \ distance from the sources and impeded settling. SPM levels will\\n                be monitored to evaluate\
      \ the dust impact during the construction phase of the Project.\\n\\n          47.4.3   The  Air  Quality\
      \  Monitoring and  Control Plan (AMCP)  in  contract-specific  Site\\n                   Environmental Plan\
      \ prepared by the Contractor shall establish procedures to monitor\\n                  impact air quality\
      \ and measures to control air pollution including dust suppression due\\n                      to construction\
      \ activities at work sites. This plan shall contain description of activities\\n                      that\
      \  will cause degradation  in  air  quality, environmental procedures  to manage\\n                     pollutants\
      \ to minimise the  air  pollution, monitoring program, record keeping and\\n                      reporting.\\\
      n\\n          47.4.4   The Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks specified, and will inspect\\\
      n                  necessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of air quality given in\\\
      n               AMCP.\\n          47.4.5    Information gathered during the AMCP  will be catalogued and maintained\
      \ by the\\n                    Contractor and shall be available for review by the Engineer.\\n\\n       \
      \   47.4.6   The exact location of the air monitoring stations located near air sensitive receptors\\n   \
      \                 adjoining the construction sites, such as residences, schools, hotels and hospitals and\\\
      n                  placement of monitoring equipment thereat shall be agreed with the Engineer prior to\\\
      n               commencement of air monitoring program.\\n\\n          47.4.7   Impact monitoring during the\
      \ course  of the Works  shall be carried out  at the\\n                    monitoring stations for two days\
      \ (continuous twenty-four hours) every fifteen days and\\n                 where there is a perceived air\
      \ quality problem.\\n          47.4.8   The Contractor shall construct suitable fence, lockable gate, 220V\
      \ AC power point and\\n                     suitable access at each air monitoring station.  Monitoring stations\
      \ shall be free from\\n                      local obstructions or sheltering.\\n\\n\\n          47.4.9  \
      \ Should impact monitoring record dust levels which are:\\n\\n\\n                       indicative of a deteriorating\
      \ situation such that closer monitoring is reasonably\\n                          indicated, or\\n\\n\\n \
      \                     when in the opinion of the Engineer additional measurements are required in view\\\
      n                          of deteriorating air quality,\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation\
      \ Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 71 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        50.15998077392578,\n        15.768376350402832,\n        564.21923828125,\n        24.210372924804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                \
      \                                                                                   Safety Health & Environment\
      \ (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        26.57955551147461,\n\

      \ Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                              September 2026         Section Page 71 of 114 \\\
      n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55824279785156,\n        38.09945297241211,\n      \
      \  515.8890991210938,\n        680.9317016601562\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"\
      \ \\n47.3.13 \\nThe Contractor shall design and implement his blasting techniques so as to minimise \\ndust,\
      \ noise, vibration generation and prevention fly rock. \\n \\n47.3.14 \\nBlasting technique should be consistent\
      \ not only with nature and quaintly of rock to be \\nblasted but also the location of blasting. \\n47.3.15\
      \ \\nThe contractor shall give preference to explosives with better environmental \\ncharacteristics. \\n47.3.16\
      \ \\nThe Contractor shall protect structures, utilities, pavements roads and other facilities \\nfrom disfiguration\
      \ and damage as a result of his activities. Where this is not possible, \\nthe contractor shall restore the\
      \ structures, utilities, pavements, roads and other \\nfacilities to their original or better, failing which\
      \ the rectification/restoration work shall \\nbe carried out at the risk and cost of the contractor.    \\\
      n \\n47.3.17 \\nThe Contractor shall submit to the Employer an Air Monitoring and Control Plan \\n(AMCP) under\
      \ contract specific Site Environmental Plan to guide construction activity \\ninsofar as it relates to monitoring,\
      \ controlling and mitigating air pollution. \\n \\n47.4         AIR MONITORING \\n47.4.1 \\nConstruction activities\
      \ that will generate dust impacts include excavation (including \\nrelated activities), material handling\
      \ and stockpiling, vehicular movement, and wind \\nerosion of unpaved work areas.  \\n \\n47.4.2 The impact\
      \ of fugitive dust on ambient air pollution depends on the quantity generated, as \\nwell as the drift potential\
      \ of the dust particles injected into the atmosphere.  Large dust \\nparticles will settle out near the source\
      \ and smaller particles are likely to undergo \\ndispersal over greater distance from the sources and impeded\
      \ settling. SPM levels will \\nbe monitored to evaluate the dust impact during the construction phase of the\
      \ Project. \\n \\n47.4.3 \\nThe Air Quality Monitoring and Control Plan (AMCP) in contract-specific Site \\\
      nEnvironmental Plan prepared by the Contractor shall establish procedures to monitor \\nimpact air quality\
      \ and measures to control air pollution including dust suppression due \\nto construction activities at work\
      \ sites. This plan shall contain description of activities \\nthat will cause degradation in air quality,\
      \ environmental procedures to manage \\npollutants to minimise the air pollution, monitoring program, record\
      \ keeping and \\nreporting.   \\n \\n47.4.4 \\nThe Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks\
      \ specified, and will inspect \\nnecessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of air quality\
      \ given in \\nAMCP. \\n47.4.5 \\nInformation gathered during the AMCP will be catalogued and maintained by\
      \ the \\nContractor and shall be available for review by the Engineer. \\n \\n47.4.6 \\nThe exact location\
      \ of the air monitoring stations located near air sensitive receptors \\nadjoining the construction sites,\
      \ such as residences, schools, hotels and hospitals and \\nplacement of monitoring equipment thereat shall\
      \ be agreed with the Engineer prior to \\ncommencement of air monitoring program.  \\n \\n47.4.7 \\nImpact\
      \ monitoring during the course of the Works shall be carried out at the \\nmonitoring stations for two days\
      \ (continuous twenty-four hours) every fifteen days and \\nwhere there is a perceived air quality problem.\
      \   \\n47.4.8 \\nThe Contractor shall construct suitable fence, lockable gate, 220V AC power point and \\\
      nsuitable access at each air monitoring station.  Monitoring stations shall be free from \\nlocal obstructions\
      \ or sheltering. \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55990600585938,\n        695.0992431640625,\n\
      \        375.6074523925781,\n        704.4517211914062\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"47.4.9 \\nShould impact monitoring record dust levels which are: \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        130.5602264404297,\n        719.2191772460938,\n        515.8877563476562,\n        740.0916748046875\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 5,\n      \"text\": \" \\nindicative of a deteriorating situation such\
      \ that closer monitoring is reasonably \\nindicated, or \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        130.5609130859375,\n\

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 76,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        260\\n        Then the Engineer may require the Contractor\
      \ to increase the frequency of impact\\n          monitoring at any one or more of the monitoring stations\
      \ until the results indicate an\\n         improving and acceptable level of air quality.\\n\\n\\n47.4.10\
      \  The Contractor shall keep records of air quality monitoring (including location, date,\\n           time).\
      \ The Contractor shall submit a copy of monitoring results to the Engineer. The\\n           results should\
      \ represent a  statistical evaluation of data by calculating maximum,\\n        minimum, mean, standard deviation,\
      \ geometric mean and percentile calculations for\\n          evaluation of frequency distribution, trends,\
      \ and comparison with emission standards.\\n\\n\\n47.4.11  The National Ambient Air Quality Standards given\
      \ in Air (Prevention and Control of\\n           Pollution) Act, 1981 may be referred by the Contractor for\
      \ Limit Levels of SPM in\\n         ambient  air which may be followed  in estimating the  pollution  level\
      \ caused by\\n          Contractor’s activities.\\n\\n47.4.12  Where  the  Engineer  determines  that  the\
      \  recorded  dust  (TSP)  level  is\\n          significantly  greater  than  the  Limit  levels,  the Engineer\
      \ may  direct  the\\n         Contractor to take effective remedial measures including, but not limited to,\\\
      n         reviewing dust sources and modifying working procedures.\\n\\n47.4.13  Where the recorded baseline\
      \ levels exceed the ambient air quality standards, then at\\n         such locations the action level is the\
      \ recorded base line. Contractor shall take all\\n           effective remedial measures to contain the levels\
      \ to their baseline value as a result of\\n           his activities. The action level may be varied by and\
      \ at the sole discretion of the\\n         Engineer.\\n\\n47.4.14 The Contractor shall inform the Engineer\
      \ of  all steps taken to investigate cause of\\n        exceedance and immediate action taken to avoid further\
      \ exceedance through written\\n         reports and proposals for action under an Event Contingency Plan.\\\
      n\\n\\n\\n48.0     Water Quality\\n\\n\\n48.1    The Contractor shall comply with the Indian Government legislation\
      \ and other State\\n          regulations in existence in Delhi insofar as they relate to water pollution\
      \ control and\\n          monitoring. A drainage system should be constructed at the commencement of the\\\
      n         Works, to drain off all surface water from the work site into suitable drain outlet.\\n\\n\\n48.2\
      \    The Contractor shall provide adequate precautions to ensure that no spoil or debris of\\n        any\
      \ kind is pushed, washed, falls or deposited on land adjacent to the site perimeter\\n          including\
      \ public roads or existing stream courses and drains within or adjacent to the\\n            site. In the\
      \ event of any spoil or debris from construction works being deposited or any\\n               silt washed\
      \ down to any area, then all such spoil, debris or material and silt shall be\\n         immediately removed\
      \ and the affected land and areas restored to their natural state by\\n          the Contractor to the satisfaction\
      \ of the Employer.\\n\\n\\n48.3    Due to lowering of potable water supplies in Bangalore and subsequent contamination\\\
      n           of ground water, the Contractor is not allowed to discharge water from the site without\\n   \
      \       the approval of the Employer. The Contractor must comply with the requirements of\\n          the\
      \ Local Ground Water Board for discharge of water arising from dewatering. Any\\n         water obtained from\
      \ dewatering systems installed in the works must be either re-used\\n            for construction purposes\
      \ and this water may subsequently be discharged to the\\n         drainage system or,  if not re-used, recharged\
      \ to the ground water at suitable aquifer\\n           levels. The Contractor must submit his proposals for\
      \ approval of Employer, on his\\n         proposed locations of dewatering of excavation and collection of\
      \ water for either\\n          construction re-use  or recharge  directly  to  aquifers. The  Contractor’s\
      \ recharge\\n         proposals must be sufficient for recharging of the quantity of water remaining after\\\
      n         deduction of water re-used for construction. During dewatering, the contractor shall\\n        \
      \ monitor ground water levels from wells to ensure that draw down levels do not exceed\\n          allowable\
      \ limits.  The Contractor will not be permitted to directly discharge, to the\\n         drainage system,\
      \ unused ground  water  obtaining  from  the  excavation  without\\n          obtaining approval of Employer\
      \ or the Agency controlling the system.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.           \
      \   September 2026     Section Page 72 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n       \

      : \"47.4.10 \\nThe Contractor shall keep records of air quality monitoring (including location, date, \\ntime).\
      \ The Contractor shall submit a copy of monitoring results to the Engineer. The \\nresults should represent\
      \ a statistical evaluation of data by calculating maximum, \\nminimum, mean, standard deviation, geometric\
      \ mean and percentile calculations for \\nevaluation of frequency distribution, trends, and comparison with\
      \ emission standards. \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.43743133544922,\n        154.13949584960938,\n\
      \        512.7681884765625,\n        785.4517822265625\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 6,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"47.4.11 \\nThe National Ambient Air Quality Standards given in Air (Prevention and Control of \\nPollution)\
      \ Act, 1981 may be referred by the Contractor for Limit Levels of SPM in \\nambient air which may be followed\
      \ in estimating the pollution level caused by \\nContractor’s activities.   \\n \\n47.4.12 \\nWhere the Engineer\
      \ determines that the recorded dust (TSP) level is \\nsignificantly greater than the Limit levels, the Engineer\
      \ may direct the \\nContractor to take effective remedial measures including, but not limited to, \\nreviewing\
      \ dust sources and modifying working procedures. \\n \\n47.4.13   Where the recorded baseline levels exceed\
      \ the ambient air quality standards, then at \\nsuch locations the action level is the recorded base line.\
      \ Contractor shall take all \\neffective remedial measures to contain the levels to their baseline value as\
      \ a result of \\nhis activities. The action level may be varied by and at the sole discretion of the \\nEngineer.\
      \  \\n \\n47.4.14 The Contractor shall inform the Engineer of all steps taken to investigate cause of \\nexceedance\
      \ and immediate action taken to avoid further exceedance through written \\nreports and proposals for action\
      \ under an Event Contingency Plan. \\n \\n \\n48.0 \\nWater Quality \\n \\n48.1  \\nThe Contractor shall comply\
      \ with the Indian Government legislation and other State \\nregulations in existence in Delhi insofar as they\
      \ relate to water pollution control and \\nmonitoring. A drainage system should be constructed at the commencement\
      \ of the \\nWorks, to drain off all surface water from the work site into suitable drain outlet. \\n \\n48.2\
      \  \\nThe Contractor shall provide adequate precautions to ensure that no spoil or debris of \\nany kind is\
      \ pushed, washed, falls or deposited on land adjacent to the site perimeter \\nincluding public roads or existing\
      \ stream courses and drains within or adjacent to the \\nsite. In the event of any spoil or debris from construction\
      \ works being deposited or any \\nsilt washed down to any area, then all such spoil, debris or material and\
      \ silt shall be \\nimmediately removed and the affected land and areas restored to their natural state by\
      \ \\nthe Contractor to the satisfaction of the Employer. \\n \\n48.3  \\nDue to lowering of potable water\
      \ supplies in Bangalore and subsequent contamination \\nof ground water, the Contractor is not allowed to\
      \ discharge water from the site without \\nthe approval of the Employer.  The Contractor must comply with\
      \ the requirements of \\nthe Local Ground Water Board for discharge of water arising from dewatering. Any\
      \ \\nwater obtained from dewatering systems installed in the works must be either re-used \\nfor construction\
      \ purposes and this water may subsequently be discharged to the \\ndrainage system or, if not re-used, recharged\
      \ to the ground water at suitable aquifer \\nlevels.  The Contractor must submit his proposals for approval\
      \ of Employer, on his \\nproposed locations of dewatering of excavation and collection of water for either\
      \ \\nconstruction re-use or recharge directly to aquifers. The Contractor’s recharge \\nproposals must be\
      \ sufficient for recharging of the quantity of water remaining after \\ndeduction of water re-used for construction.\
      \ During dewatering, the contractor shall \\nmonitor ground water levels from wells to ensure that draw down\
      \ levels do not exceed \\nallowable limits.  The Contractor will not be permitted to directly discharge, to\
      \ the \\ndrainage system, unused ground water obtaining from the excavation without \\nobtaining approval\
      \ of Employer or the Agency controlling the system. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        546.0439453125,\n\

    the Site Environmental Plan to address exceedances, fugitive dust suppression, and equipment emissions.

      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n257\\\
      n                           PART- IV ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT\\n\\n             47.0 ENVIORNMENTAL MANAGEMENT\\\
      n\\n             47.1 ENVIRONMENTAL MONITORING\\n\\n              47.1.1 The  Contractor’s  Environmental\
      \  Team  shall  carry  out  the  monitoring  of\\n                     environmental impacts during construction.\
      \ Representative sensitive receivers in the\\n                           vicinity of the works shall be monitored\
      \ for noise and air quality impacts.\\n              47.1.2  For carrying out impact monitoring for noise\
      \ and air, equipment shall be provided,\\n                    operated and maintained by the Contractor. The\
      \ equipment shall be kept in a good\\n                       state  of  repair  in accordance  with  the \
      \ manufacturer’s recommendations and\\n                    maintained in proper working order with sufficient\
      \ spare equipment available in the\\n                     event of breakdown to maintain the planned monitoring\
      \ program.\\n              47.1.2 The calibration of monitoring instruments and their respective calibrators\
      \ shall be\\n                       carried out in accordance with the manufacturer’s requirements to ensure\
      \ they\\n                    perform to the same level of accuracy as stated in the manufacturer’s specifications.\\\
      n              47.1.3 Suspended Particulate Matter (SPM) levels shall be measured by following the\\n    \
      \                 standard high volume sampling method as set out in High Volume Method for\\n           \
      \       Suspended Particulate, BIS: 5182-1981\\n              47.1.4  24-hour average SPM concentration shall\
      \ be measured by drawing air through a\\n                    High Volume Sampler (HVS) fitted with pre-weighted\
      \ Glass Fiber filter paper at an\\n                   average flow rate not less than 1.1m3 per minute.\\\
      n              47.1.5 The minimum requirements to the specifications of sound level meter    should be\\n\
      \                   as given in IS: 9779-1981\\n              47.1.6  Engineer will undertake baseline monitoring\
      \ to establish background levels. Action\\n                      Level of the Contractor shall be based on\
      \ the results of baseline monitoring\\n                    program, which will be made available to him prior\
      \ to start of construction.\\n               47.1.7 The Contractor’s monitoring program is summarized in Table\
      \ –1.\\n\\n                                       TABLE-1\\n\\n                SUMMARY OF CONTRACTOR’S MONITORING\
      \ PROGRAMME\\n                Parameter                   Noise                              Air\\n\\n   \
      \       Sampling                Day Time (6.00 to 22.00 hrs):   RSPM, SPM\\n                             \
      \                Lmax, Leq, L10, L90, L50            24-hours of the day\\n                              \
      \          Night Time (9 PM – 6AM):      CO: 12 hrs from 8.00 to 20.00\\n                                \
      \             Lmax, Leq, L10, L90, L50, Ldn          hrs.\\n\\n\\n          Frequency               Once a\
      \ week (when noise     Two 24-hour Samples every\\n           At each location              generating activities\
      \ are           fifteen days at uniform intervals.\\n                                      underway).\\n \
      \          Locations                To  be  determined  by  the  To be determined by the\\n              \
      \                          Contractor  based  on  noise  Contractor based on air sensitive\\n            \
      \                               sensitive receptors.              receptors.\\n         Number of Locations\
      \        4 Locations                  2 Locations\\n           Duration  of  Monitoring by  During Civil Construction\
      \        During Civil Construction\\n           Contractor\\n            Additional Requirements     Adhoc\
      \ monitoring as required.   Ad hoc monitoring as required\\n\\n\\n        47.2EVENT CONTINGENCY PLAN\\n  \
      \      The Contractor shall prepare an Event Contingency Plan under his Site Environmental Plan. The\\n  \
      \       purpose is to provide, in addition to monitoring activities, procedures for ensuring that  if any\\\
      n          environmental  exceedance  of  limiting  values  (either  accidental  or  through  inadequate\\\
      n          implementation of mitigation measures on part of the Contractor) does occur, the cause is\\n  \
      \        quickly identified and remedied, and that the risk of a similar event recurring is reduced.\\n\\\
      n\\n          47.3      Air Quality\\n\\n\\n          47.3.1   The  Contractor  shall  take  all necessary\
      \  precautions  to minimise  fugitive  dust\\n                  emissions from operations involving excavation,\
      \ grading, and clearing of land and\\n                    disposal of waste. He shall not allow emissions\
      \ of fugitive dust from any transport,\\n                    handling, construction or storage activity to\
      \ remain visible in atmosphere beyond the\\n                    property line of emission source for any prolonged\
      \ period of time without notification to\\n                    the Employer.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore\

      : 11,\n      \"text\": \"Sampling  \\nDay Time (6.00 to 22.00 hrs): \\nLmax, Leq, L10, L90, L50 \\nNight Time\
      \ (9 PM – 6AM):  \\nLmax, Leq, L10, L90, L50, Ldn \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        374.3998107910156,\n\
      \        401.2193603515625,\n        528.3670654296875,\n        445.0117492675781\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 12,\n      \"text\": \"RSPM, SPM  \\n24-hours of the day  \\nCO: 12 hrs from 8.00 to 20.00 \\nhrs.  \\n\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        90.83509826660156,\n        465.059326171875,\n        164.76719665527344,\n\
      \        485.9317626953125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 13,\n      \"text\": \"Frequency \\nAt each\
      \ location \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        225.840087890625,\n        465.059326171875,\n\
      \        343.5673522949219,\n        497.4517517089844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 14,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"Once a week (when noise \\ngenerating activities are \\nunderway). \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        90.83995819091797,\n        465.0592956542969,\n        521.1668701171875,\n        532.49169921875\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 15,\n      \"text\": \"Two 24-hour Samples every \\nfifteen days at uniform\
      \ intervals.  \\n \\nLocations \\nTo be determined by the \\nContractor based on noise \\nsensitive receptors.\
      \  \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        90.83995819091797,\n        500.0992736816406,\n     \
      \   523.20849609375,\n        567.8917236328125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 16,\n      \"text\": \"\
      To be determined by the \\nContractor based on air sensitive \\nreceptors. \\nNumber of Locations \\n 4 Locations\
      \ \\n  2 Locations \\nDuration of Monitoring by \\nContractor \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n  \
      \      225.83995056152344,\n        547.1392822265625,\n        487.2071533203125,\n        556.4917602539062\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 17,\n      \"text\": \"During Civil Construction \\nDuring Civil Construction\
      \ \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55929565429688,\n        570.5392456054688,\n      \
      \  515.8875732421875,\n        787.9716796875\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 18,\n      \"text\": \"Additional\
      \ Requirements \\nAdhoc monitoring as required. \\nAd hoc monitoring as required \\n \\n47.2EVENT CONTINGENCY\
      \ PLAN \\nThe Contractor shall prepare an Event Contingency Plan under his Site Environmental Plan. The \\\
      npurpose is to provide, in addition to monitoring activities, procedures for ensuring that if any \\nenvironmental\
      \ exceedance of limiting values (either accidental or through inadequate \\nimplementation of mitigation measures\
      \ on part of the Contractor) does occur, the cause is \\nquickly identified and remedied, and that the risk\
      \ of a similar event recurring is reduced.  \\n \\n47.3 \\nAir Quality  \\n \\n47.3.1 \\nThe Contractor shall\
      \ take all necessary precautions to minimise fugitive dust \\nemissions from operations involving excavation,\
      \ grading, and clearing of land and \\ndisposal of waste. He shall not allow emissions of fugitive dust from\
      \ any transport, \\nhandling, construction or storage activity to remain visible in atmosphere beyond the\
      \ \\nproperty line of emission source for any prolonged period of time without notification to \\nthe Employer.\
      \ \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        39.599998474121094,\n        24.479991912841797,\n\
      \        63.57600021362305,\n        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 19,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"257\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}"

    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4.md
    okf_sha256: 8a9850438ec160be318ccdedeb8465b51ba9850986edff681868f388391ea34a
    heading: 'Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management'
    source_document: Vol3DLNP1.pdf
    source_sha256: 1ee3fc8dfd12f659f34446b0a6db74e1e1cdd4d593ca91b2a48d59bf1ef97d52

    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/part_4_environmental_management_corrected/environment_clauses.json
    json_sha256: 7bea653d28db3a09695fc577706045a118cd6f049b1a965a72f0af31b2b73dbf

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 74,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        258\\n47.3.2   The Contractor shall use construction\
      \ equipment designed and equipped to minimise\\n          or control air pollution. He shall maintain evidence\
      \ of such design and equipment and\\n       make these available for inspection by Employer.\\n\\n\\n47.3.3\
      \      If  after  commencement  of  construction  activity,  Employer  believes  that  the\\n          Contractor’s\
      \ equipment or methods of working are causing unacceptable air pollution\\n         impacts then these shall\
      \ be inspected and remedial proposals shall be drawn up by\\n          the Contractor, submitted for review\
      \ to the Employer and implemented.\\n\\n\\n47.3.4    In developing these remedial measures, the Contractor\
      \ shall inspect and review all\\n          dust sources that may be contributing to air pollution. Remedial\
      \ measures include use\\n           of additional / alternative equipment by the Contractor or maintenance\
      \ / modification of\\n           existing equipment of the Contractor.\\n\\n           In the event that approved\
      \ remedial measures are not being implemented and serious\\n         impacts persist, the Employer may direct\
      \ the Contractor to suspend work until the\\n        measures are implemented, as required under the Contract.\\\
      n\\n\\n47.3.5    Contractor’s  transport  vehicles and  other equipment  shall conform  to emission\\n   \
      \      standards  fixed by  Statutory  Agencies  of Government  of  India  or  the  State\\n        Government\
      \ from time to time. The Contractor shall carry out periodical checks and\\n         undertake remedial measures\
      \ including replacement,  if required, so as to operate\\n           within permissible norms.\\n\\n\\n47.3.6\
      \   The Contractor shall establish and maintain records of routine maintenance program\\n            for internal\
      \ combustion engine powered vehicles and equipment used on this project.\\n       He shall keep records available\
      \ for inspection by Employer.\\n\\n\\n47.3.7   The Contractor shall cover loads of dust generating materials\
      \ like debris and soil being\\n          transported from construction  sites.  All trucks carrying loose\
      \ material should be\\n         covered and loaded with sufficient free- board to avoid spills through the\
      \ tailboard or\\n          sideboards.\\n\\n\\n47.3.8   The Contractor shall promptly transport all excavation\
      \ disposal materials of whatever\\n          kind so as not to delay work on the project. Stockpiling of materials\
      \  will only be\\n         allowed at sites designated by the Employer. The Contractor shall place excavation\\\
      n          materials in the dumping/disposal areas designated in the plans as given in the\\n           specifications.\\\
      n\\n\\n47.3.9   The temporary dumping areas shall be maintained by the Contractor at all times until\\n  \
      \        the excavate is re-utilised for backfilling or as directed by Employer. Dust control\\n         \
      \   activities shall continue even during any work stoppage.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n47.3.10  The Contractor shall place\
      \ material in a manner that will minimize dust production.\\n          Material shall be minimized each day\
      \ and wetted, to minimize dust production. During\\n          dry weather, dust control methods must be used\
      \ daily especially on windy, dry days to\\n         prevent any dust from blowing across the site perimeter.\\\
      n\\n\\n47.3.11  The Contractor shall water down construction sites as required to suppress dust,\\n      \
      \    during handling of excavation soil or debris or during demolition. The Contractor will\\n       make\
      \ water sprinklers, water supply and water delivering equipment available at any\\n         time that it is\
      \ required for dust control use. Dust screens will be used, as feasible when\\n           additional dust\
      \  control measures are needed  specially where the work  is near\\n          sensitive receptors.\\n\\n\\\
      n47.3.12  The Contractor shall provide a wash pit or a wheel washing and/or vehicle cleaning\\n          \
      \   facility at the exits from work sites such as construction depots and batching plants. At\\n         such\
      \ facility, high-pressure water jets will be directed at the wheels of vehicles to\\n        remove all spoil\
      \ and dirt.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page\
      \ 70 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        47.039981842041016,\n        15.768376350402832,\n\
      \        561.0993041992188,\n        24.210372924804688\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"                                                                                                     \
      \                              Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        26.57955551147461,\n        85.20714569091797,\n        35.93199157714844\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\": \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n      \
      \  82.4399642944336,\n        809.042724609375,\n        508.6270751953125,\n        830.5714111328125\n \
      \     ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.            \
      \                  September 2026         Section Page 70 of 114 \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        38.09945297241211,\n        515.5282592773438,\n        780.4119262695312\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"47.3.2 \\nThe Contractor shall use construction equipment\
      \ designed and equipped to minimise \\nor control air pollution. He shall maintain evidence of such design\
      \ and equipment and \\nmake these available for inspection by Employer. \\n \\n47.3.3 \\nIf after commencement\
      \ of construction activity, Employer believes that the \\nContractor’s equipment or methods of working are\
      \ causing unacceptable air pollution \\nimpacts then these shall be inspected and remedial proposals shall\
      \ be drawn up by \\nthe Contractor, submitted for review to the Employer and implemented.  \\n \\n47.3.4 \\\
      nIn developing these remedial measures, the Contractor shall inspect and review all \\ndust sources that may\
      \ be contributing to air pollution. Remedial measures include use \\nof additional / alternative equipment\
      \ by the Contractor or maintenance / modification of \\nexisting equipment of the Contractor. \\nIn the event\
      \ that approved remedial measures are not being implemented and serious \\nimpacts persist, the Employer may\
      \ direct the Contractor to suspend work until the \\nmeasures are implemented, as required under the Contract.\
      \ \\n \\n47.3.5 \\nContractor’s transport vehicles and other equipment shall conform to emission \\nstandards\
      \ fixed by Statutory Agencies of Government of India or the State \\nGovernment from time to time.  The Contractor\
      \ shall carry out periodical checks and \\nundertake remedial measures including replacement, if required,\
      \ so as to operate \\nwithin permissible norms.   \\n \\n47.3.6 \\nThe Contractor shall establish and maintain\
      \ records of routine maintenance program \\nfor internal combustion engine powered vehicles and equipment\
      \ used on this project.  \\nHe shall keep records available for inspection by Employer. \\n \\n47.3.7 \\nThe\
      \ Contractor shall cover loads of dust generating materials like debris and soil being \\ntransported from\
      \ construction sites. All trucks carrying loose material should be \\ncovered and loaded with sufficient free-\
      \ board to avoid spills through the tailboard or \\nsideboards. \\n \\n47.3.8 \\nThe Contractor shall promptly\
      \ transport all excavation disposal materials of whatever \\nkind so as not to delay work on the project.\
      \ Stockpiling of materials will only be \\nallowed at sites designated by the Employer. The Contractor shall\
      \ place excavation \\nmaterials in the dumping/disposal areas designated in the plans as given in the \\nspecifications.\
      \  \\n \\n47.3.9  \\nThe temporary dumping areas shall be maintained by the Contractor at all times until\
      \ \\nthe excavate is re-utilised for backfilling or as directed by Employer. Dust control \\nactivities shall\
      \ continue even during any work stoppage. \\n \\n \\n47.3.10 \\nThe Contractor shall place material in a manner\
      \ that will minimize dust production.  \\nMaterial shall be minimized each day and wetted, to minimize dust\
      \ production.  During \\ndry weather, dust control methods must be used daily especially on windy, dry days\
      \ to \\nprevent any dust from blowing across the site perimeter. \\n \\n47.3.11 \\nThe Contractor shall water\
      \ down construction sites as required to suppress dust, \\nduring handling of excavation soil or debris or\
      \ during demolition. The Contractor will \\nmake water sprinklers, water supply and water delivering equipment\
      \ available at any \\ntime that it is required for dust control use. Dust screens will be used, as feasible\
      \ when \\nadditional dust control measures are needed specially where the work is near \\nsensitive receptors.\
      \ \\n \\n47.3.12 \\nThe Contractor shall provide a wash pit or a wheel washing and/or vehicle cleaning \\\
      nfacility at the exits from work sites such as construction depots and batching plants. At \\nsuch facility,\
      \ high-pressure water jets will be directed at the wheels of vehicles to \\nremove all spoil and dirt.  \\\
      n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        546.0439453125,\n        24.479991912841797,\n        570.0199584960938,\n\
      \        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\": \"258\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n\
      }"

    okf_path: OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4.md
    okf_sha256: 8a9850438ec160be318ccdedeb8465b51ba9850986edff681868f388391ea34a

    json_path: parsed_json/vol3/part_4_environmental_management_corrected/environment_clauses.json

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 75,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n259\\\
      n\\n          47.3.13  The Contractor shall design and implement his blasting techniques so as to minimise\\\
      n                     dust, noise, vibration generation and prevention fly rock.\\n\\n\\n          47.3.14\
      \   Blasting technique should be consistent not only with nature and quaintly of rock to be\\n           \
      \         blasted but also the location of blasting.\\n\\n          47.3.15  The  contractor  shall  give\
      \  preference  to  explosives  with  better  environmental\\n                      characteristics.\\n\\n\
      \          47.3.16  The Contractor shall protect structures, utilities, pavements roads and other facilities\\\
      n                  from disfiguration and damage as a result of his activities. Where this is not possible,\\\
      n                    the contractor  shall restore the structures,  utilities, pavements, roads and other\\\
      n                          facilities to their original or better, failing which the rectification/restoration\
      \ work shall\\n                be carried out at the risk and cost of the contractor.\\n\\n\\n          47.3.17\
      \  The Contractor shall submit to the Employer an Air Monitoring and Control Plan\\n               (AMCP)\
      \ under contract specific Site Environmental Plan to guide construction activity\\n                     insofar\
      \ as it relates to monitoring, controlling and mitigating air pollution.\\n\\n\\n          47.4    AIR MONITORING\\\
      n          47.4.1    Construction activities that will generate dust impacts include excavation (including\\\
      n                     related activities), material handling and stockpiling, vehicular movement, and wind\\\
      n                   erosion of unpaved work areas.\\n\\n          47.4.2 The impact of fugitive dust on ambient\
      \ air pollution depends on the quantity generated, as\\n                     well as the drift potential of\
      \ the dust particles injected into the atmosphere. Large dust\\n                      particles will settle\
      \ out near the source and smaller particles are likely to undergo\\n                    dispersal over greater\
      \ distance from the sources and impeded settling. SPM levels will\\n                be monitored to evaluate\
      \ the dust impact during the construction phase of the Project.\\n\\n          47.4.3   The  Air  Quality\
      \  Monitoring and  Control Plan (AMCP)  in  contract-specific  Site\\n                   Environmental Plan\
      \ prepared by the Contractor shall establish procedures to monitor\\n                  impact air quality\
      \ and measures to control air pollution including dust suppression due\\n                      to construction\
      \ activities at work sites. This plan shall contain description of activities\\n                      that\
      \  will cause degradation  in  air  quality, environmental procedures  to manage\\n                     pollutants\
      \ to minimise the  air  pollution, monitoring program, record keeping and\\n                      reporting.\\\
      n\\n          47.4.4   The Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks specified, and will inspect\\\
      n                  necessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of air quality given in\\\
      n               AMCP.\\n          47.4.5    Information gathered during the AMCP  will be catalogued and maintained\
      \ by the\\n                    Contractor and shall be available for review by the Engineer.\\n\\n       \
      \   47.4.6   The exact location of the air monitoring stations located near air sensitive receptors\\n   \
      \                 adjoining the construction sites, such as residences, schools, hotels and hospitals and\\\
      n                  placement of monitoring equipment thereat shall be agreed with the Engineer prior to\\\
      n               commencement of air monitoring program.\\n\\n          47.4.7   Impact monitoring during the\
      \ course  of the Works  shall be carried out  at the\\n                    monitoring stations for two days\
      \ (continuous twenty-four hours) every fifteen days and\\n                 where there is a perceived air\
      \ quality problem.\\n          47.4.8   The Contractor shall construct suitable fence, lockable gate, 220V\
      \ AC power point and\\n                     suitable access at each air monitoring station.  Monitoring stations\
      \ shall be free from\\n                      local obstructions or sheltering.\\n\\n\\n          47.4.9  \
      \ Should impact monitoring record dust levels which are:\\n\\n\\n                       indicative of a deteriorating\
      \ situation such that closer monitoring is reasonably\\n                          indicated, or\\n\\n\\n \
      \                     when in the opinion of the Engineer additional measurements are required in view\\\
      n                          of deteriorating air quality,\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation\
      \ Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 71 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        50.15998077392578,\n        15.768376350402832,\n        564.21923828125,\n        24.210372924804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                \
      \                                                                                   Safety Health & Environment\
      \ (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        26.57955551147461,\n\
      \        88.32714080810547,\n        35.93199157714844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        809.042724609375,\n     \
      \   511.7470703125,\n        830.5714111328125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore\
      \ Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                              September 2026         Section Page 71 of 114 \\\
      n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55824279785156,\n        38.09945297241211,\n      \
      \  515.8890991210938,\n        680.9317016601562\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"\
      \ \\n47.3.13 \\nThe Contractor shall design and implement his blasting techniques so as to minimise \\ndust,\
      \ noise, vibration generation and prevention fly rock. \\n \\n47.3.14 \\nBlasting technique should be consistent\
      \ not only with nature and quaintly of rock to be \\nblasted but also the location of blasting. \\n47.3.15\
      \ \\nThe contractor shall give preference to explosives with better environmental \\ncharacteristics. \\n47.3.16\
      \ \\nThe Contractor shall protect structures, utilities, pavements roads and other facilities \\nfrom disfiguration\
      \ and damage as a result of his activities. Where this is not possible, \\nthe contractor shall restore the\
      \ structures, utilities, pavements, roads and other \\nfacilities to their original or better, failing which\
      \ the rectification/restoration work shall \\nbe carried out at the risk and cost of the contractor.    \\\
      n \\n47.3.17 \\nThe Contractor shall submit to the Employer an Air Monitoring and Control Plan \\n(AMCP) under\
      \ contract specific Site Environmental Plan to guide construction activity \\ninsofar as it relates to monitoring,\
      \ controlling and mitigating air pollution. \\n \\n47.4         AIR MONITORING \\n47.4.1 \\nConstruction activities\
      \ that will generate dust impacts include excavation (including \\nrelated activities), material handling\
      \ and stockpiling, vehicular movement, and wind \\nerosion of unpaved work areas.  \\n \\n47.4.2 The impact\
      \ of fugitive dust on ambient air pollution depends on the quantity generated, as \\nwell as the drift potential\
      \ of the dust particles injected into the atmosphere.  Large dust \\nparticles will settle out near the source\
      \ and smaller particles are likely to undergo \\ndispersal over greater distance from the sources and impeded\
      \ settling. SPM levels will \\nbe monitored to evaluate the dust impact during the construction phase of the\
      \ Project. \\n \\n47.4.3 \\nThe Air Quality Monitoring and Control Plan (AMCP) in contract-specific Site \\\
      nEnvironmental Plan prepared by the Contractor shall establish procedures to monitor \\nimpact air quality\
      \ and measures to control air pollution including dust suppression due \\nto construction activities at work\
      \ sites. This plan shall contain description of activities \\nthat will cause degradation in air quality,\
      \ environmental procedures to manage \\npollutants to minimise the air pollution, monitoring program, record\
      \ keeping and \\nreporting.   \\n \\n47.4.4 \\nThe Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks\
      \ specified, and will inspect \\nnecessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of air quality\
      \ given in \\nAMCP. \\n47.4.5 \\nInformation gathered during the AMCP will be catalogued and maintained by\
      \ the \\nContractor and shall be available for review by the Engineer. \\n \\n47.4.6 \\nThe exact location\
      \ of the air monitoring stations located near air sensitive receptors \\nadjoining the construction sites,\
      \ such as residences, schools, hotels and hospitals and \\nplacement of monitoring equipment thereat shall\
      \ be agreed with the Engineer prior to \\ncommencement of air monitoring program.  \\n \\n47.4.7 \\nImpact\
      \ monitoring during the course of the Works shall be carried out at the \\nmonitoring stations for two days\
      \ (continuous twenty-four hours) every fifteen days and \\nwhere there is a perceived air quality problem.\
      \   \\n47.4.8 \\nThe Contractor shall construct suitable fence, lockable gate, 220V AC power point and \\\
      nsuitable access at each air monitoring station.  Monitoring stations shall be free from \\nlocal obstructions\
      \ or sheltering. \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55990600585938,\n        695.0992431640625,\n\
      \        375.6074523925781,\n        704.4517211914062\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"47.4.9 \\nShould impact monitoring record dust levels which are: \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        130.5602264404297,\n        719.2191772460938,\n        515.8877563476562,\n        740.0916748046875\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 5,\n      \"text\": \" \\nindicative of a deteriorating situation such\
      \ that closer monitoring is reasonably \\nindicated, or \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        130.5609130859375,\n\
      \        754.8591918945312,\n        515.8884887695312,\n        775.7316284179688\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 6,\n      \"text\": \" \\nwhen in the opinion of the Engineer additional measurements are required in view\
      \ \\nof deteriorating air quality, \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        39.599998474121094,\n\
      \        24.479991912841797,\n        63.57600021362305,\n        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\
      : 7,\n      \"text\": \"259\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n}"

- kind: summary
  text: Water quality and dewatering provisions prohibit direct unapproved discharge of groundwater or site runoff,
    require treatment of mud slurry from drilling or tunnelling, mandate oil interceptors for workshops, and require
    compliance with local ground water board regulations.

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 77,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n261\\\
      n          48.4    The Contractor shall ensure that earth, bentonite, chemicals and concrete agitator\\n \
      \                 washings etc. are not deposited in the watercourses but are suitably collected and\\n  \
      \                 residue disposed off in a manner approved by local authorities.\\n\\n\\n          48.5 \
      \      All water and waste products (surface runoff and wastewater) arising on the site shall\\n         \
      \       be  collected and removed from  the  site  via a  suitable and properly designed\\n              \
      \    temporary drainage system and disposed off at a location and in a manner that will\\n               \
      \  cause neither pollution nor nuisance.\\n\\n\\n          48.6    Any mud slurry from drilling, tunnelling,\
      \ diaphragm wall construction or grouting etc.\\n                      shall not be discharged into the drainage\
      \ system unless treatment is carried out that\\n                          will remove silt, mud particles,\
      \ bentonite etc. The Contractor shall provide treatment\\n                          facilities as necessary\
      \ to prevent the discharge of contaminated ground water.\\n\\n\\n          48.7    The Contractor shall discharge\
      \ wastewater arising out of site office, canteen or toilet\\n                          facilities constructed\
      \ by him into sewers after obtaining prior approval of agency\\n                      controlling the system.\
      \  A wastewater drainage system shall be provided to drain\\n                  wastewater into the sewerage\
      \ system.\\n\\n\\n          48.8    The bentonite mixing, treatment and handling system shall be established\
      \ by the\\n                     contractor giving due regard to its environmental impacts. The disposal of\
      \ redundant\\n                    bentonite shall be carefully considered whether in bulk or liquid form.\
      \ The disposal\\n                     location will be advised and agreed with the relevant authorities.\\\
      n\\n\\n          48.9    The Contractor shall take measures to prevent discharge of oil and grease during\\\
      n                     spillage from reaching drainage system or any water body. Oil removal / interceptors\\\
      n                      shall be provided to treat oil waste from workshop areas etc.\\n\\n         48.10 \
      \  The Contractor shall apply to the appropriate authority for installing bore wells for\\n              \
      \     water supply at site.\\n\\n\\n          49.0     Archaeological and Historical Preservation\\n\\n  \
      \        49.1    The contractor shall seek to accommodate archaeological and historical preservation\\n  \
      \                concerns that may arise due to the construction of the project especially in close\\n   \
      \                     vicinity of such areas where such monuments may be located.\\n\\n\\n          49.2 \
      \   The contractor shall consult the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI) and other\\n                   \
      \   parties, on the advise of the Employer, to identify and assess construction effects and\\n           \
      \      seek ways to avoid, minimize or mitigate adverse effects on such monuments.\\n\\n\\n          49.3\
      \     Adverse  effects may  include  reasonably  foreseeable  effects  caused  by  the\\n                \
      \    construction that may occur later in time, be farther removed in distance or those that\\n          \
      \               alter, howsoever temporarily, the significance of the structure.\\n\\n\\n          50.0  \
      \  Landscape and Greenery\\n\\n\\n          50.1    As far as is reasonably practicable, the Contractor shall\
      \ maintain ecological balance\\n                 by preventing deforestation and defacing of natural landscape.\
      \ In respect of ecological\\n                   balance, the Contractor shall observe the following instructions.\\\
      n\\n\\n          50.2    The Contractor  shall, so conduct his construction operations, as to prevent any\\\
      n                   avoidable destruction, scarring or defacing of natural surrounding in the vicinity of\\\
      n                   work.\\n\\n\\n          50.3    Where destruction, scarring, damage or defacing may occur\
      \ as a result of operations\\n                      relating to Permanent or Temporary works, the same shall\
      \ be repaired, replanted or\\n                   otherwise corrected at Contractor’s expense. All work areas\
      \ shall be smoothened and\\n                  graded in a manner to conform to natural appearance of the landscape\
      \ as directed by\\n                    the Employer.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation\
      \ Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 73 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\"\
      : [\n        50.15998077392578,\n        15.768376350402832,\n        564.21923828125,\n        24.210372924804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                \
      \                                                                                   Safety Health & Environment\
      \ (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        26.57955551147461,\n\
      \        88.32714080810547,\n        35.93199157714844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.5599594116211,\n        809.042724609375,\n     \
      \   511.7470703125,\n        830.5714111328125\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 2,\n      \"text\": \"Bangalore\
      \ Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                              September 2026         Section Page 73 of 114 \\\
      n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        85.55965423583984,\n        38.09945297241211,\n      \
      \  515.888916015625,\n        780.8919067382812\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 3,\n      \"text\": \"\
      48.4  \\nThe Contractor shall ensure that earth, bentonite, chemicals and concrete agitator \\nwashings etc.\
      \ are not deposited in the watercourses but are suitably collected and \\nresidue disposed off in a manner\
      \ approved by local authorities.  \\n \\n48.5  \\nAll water and waste products (surface runoff and wastewater)\
      \ arising on the site shall \\nbe collected and removed from the site via a suitable and properly designed\
      \ \\ntemporary drainage system and disposed off at a location and in a manner that will \\ncause neither pollution\
      \ nor nuisance.  \\n \\n48.6  \\nAny mud slurry from drilling, tunnelling, diaphragm wall construction or\
      \ grouting etc. \\nshall not be discharged into the drainage system unless treatment is carried out that \\\
      nwill remove silt, mud particles, bentonite etc. The Contractor shall provide treatment \\nfacilities as necessary\
      \ to prevent the discharge of contaminated ground water. \\n \\n48.7  \\nThe Contractor shall discharge wastewater\
      \ arising out of site office, canteen or toilet \\nfacilities constructed by him into sewers after obtaining\
      \ prior approval of agency \\ncontrolling the system.  A wastewater drainage system shall be provided to drain\
      \ \\nwastewater into the sewerage system.   \\n \\n48.8  \\nThe bentonite mixing, treatment and handling system\
      \ shall be established by the \\ncontractor giving due regard to its environmental impacts. The disposal of\
      \ redundant \\nbentonite shall be carefully considered whether in bulk or liquid form. The disposal \\nlocation\
      \ will be advised and agreed with the relevant authorities. \\n \\n48.9  \\nThe Contractor shall take measures\
      \ to prevent discharge of oil and grease during \\nspillage from reaching drainage system or any water body.\
      \ Oil removal / interceptors \\nshall be provided to treat oil waste from workshop areas etc. \\n48.10 \\\
      nThe Contractor shall apply to the appropriate authority for installing bore wells for \\nwater supply at\
      \ site. \\n \\n49.0  \\nArchaeological and Historical Preservation \\n49.1  \\nThe contractor shall seek to\
      \ accommodate archaeological and historical preservation \\nconcerns that may arise due to the construction\
      \ of the project especially in close \\nvicinity of such areas where such monuments may be located.  \\n \\\
      n49.2  \\nThe contractor shall consult the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI) and other \\nparties, on the\
      \ advise of the Employer, to identify and assess construction effects and \\nseek ways to avoid, minimize\
      \ or mitigate adverse effects on such monuments. \\n \\n49.3   \\nAdverse effects may include reasonably foreseeable\
      \ effects caused by the \\nconstruction that may occur later in time, be farther removed in distance or those\
      \ that \\nalter, howsoever temporarily, the significance of the structure.   \\n \\n50.0 \\nLandscape and\
      \ Greenery \\n \\n50.1  \\nAs far as is reasonably practicable, the Contractor shall maintain ecological balance\
      \ \\nby preventing deforestation and defacing of natural landscape. In respect of ecological \\nbalance, the\
      \ Contractor shall observe the following instructions. \\n \\n50.2 \\nThe Contractor shall, so conduct his\
      \ construction operations, as to prevent any \\navoidable destruction, scarring or defacing of natural surrounding\
      \ in the vicinity of \\nwork. \\n \\n50.3   \\nWhere destruction, scarring, damage or defacing may occur as\
      \ a result of operations \\nrelating to Permanent or Temporary works, the same shall be repaired, replanted\
      \ or \\notherwise corrected at Contractor’s expense. All work areas shall be smoothened and \\ngraded in a\
      \ manner to conform to natural appearance of the landscape as directed by \\nthe Employer.  \\n\"\n    },\n\
      \    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        39.599998474121094,\n        24.479991912841797,\n        63.57600021362305,\n\
      \        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\": \"261\\n\"\n    }\n  ]\n\
      }"

    quote: "{\n  \"extraction_method\": \"geometric_sort\",\n  \"has_extracted_text\": true,\n  \"page_rect\": [\n\
      \    0.0,\n    0.0,\n    595.219970703125,\n    842.0\n  ],\n  \"pdf_page\": 76,\n  \"review_status\": \"\
      pending_manual_review\",\n  \"rotation\": 0,\n  \"text\": \"Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\\n  \
      \                                                        260\\n        Then the Engineer may require the Contractor\
      \ to increase the frequency of impact\\n          monitoring at any one or more of the monitoring stations\
      \ until the results indicate an\\n         improving and acceptable level of air quality.\\n\\n\\n47.4.10\
      \  The Contractor shall keep records of air quality monitoring (including location, date,\\n           time).\
      \ The Contractor shall submit a copy of monitoring results to the Engineer. The\\n           results should\
      \ represent a  statistical evaluation of data by calculating maximum,\\n        minimum, mean, standard deviation,\
      \ geometric mean and percentile calculations for\\n          evaluation of frequency distribution, trends,\
      \ and comparison with emission standards.\\n\\n\\n47.4.11  The National Ambient Air Quality Standards given\
      \ in Air (Prevention and Control of\\n           Pollution) Act, 1981 may be referred by the Contractor for\
      \ Limit Levels of SPM in\\n         ambient  air which may be followed  in estimating the  pollution  level\
      \ caused by\\n          Contractor’s activities.\\n\\n47.4.12  Where  the  Engineer  determines  that  the\
      \  recorded  dust  (TSP)  level  is\\n          significantly  greater  than  the  Limit  levels,  the Engineer\
      \ may  direct  the\\n         Contractor to take effective remedial measures including, but not limited to,\\\
      n         reviewing dust sources and modifying working procedures.\\n\\n47.4.13  Where the recorded baseline\
      \ levels exceed the ambient air quality standards, then at\\n         such locations the action level is the\
      \ recorded base line. Contractor shall take all\\n           effective remedial measures to contain the levels\
      \ to their baseline value as a result of\\n           his activities. The action level may be varied by and\
      \ at the sole discretion of the\\n         Engineer.\\n\\n47.4.14 The Contractor shall inform the Engineer\
      \ of  all steps taken to investigate cause of\\n        exceedance and immediate action taken to avoid further\
      \ exceedance through written\\n         reports and proposals for action under an Event Contingency Plan.\\\
      n\\n\\n\\n48.0     Water Quality\\n\\n\\n48.1    The Contractor shall comply with the Indian Government legislation\
      \ and other State\\n          regulations in existence in Delhi insofar as they relate to water pollution\
      \ control and\\n          monitoring. A drainage system should be constructed at the commencement of the\\\
      n         Works, to drain off all surface water from the work site into suitable drain outlet.\\n\\n\\n48.2\
      \    The Contractor shall provide adequate precautions to ensure that no spoil or debris of\\n        any\
      \ kind is pushed, washed, falls or deposited on land adjacent to the site perimeter\\n          including\
      \ public roads or existing stream courses and drains within or adjacent to the\\n            site. In the\
      \ event of any spoil or debris from construction works being deposited or any\\n               silt washed\
      \ down to any area, then all such spoil, debris or material and silt shall be\\n         immediately removed\
      \ and the affected land and areas restored to their natural state by\\n          the Contractor to the satisfaction\
      \ of the Employer.\\n\\n\\n48.3    Due to lowering of potable water supplies in Bangalore and subsequent contamination\\\
      n           of ground water, the Contractor is not allowed to discharge water from the site without\\n   \
      \       the approval of the Employer. The Contractor must comply with the requirements of\\n          the\
      \ Local Ground Water Board for discharge of water arising from dewatering. Any\\n         water obtained from\
      \ dewatering systems installed in the works must be either re-used\\n            for construction purposes\
      \ and this water may subsequently be discharged to the\\n         drainage system or,  if not re-used, recharged\
      \ to the ground water at suitable aquifer\\n           levels. The Contractor must submit his proposals for\
      \ approval of Employer, on his\\n         proposed locations of dewatering of excavation and collection of\
      \ water for either\\n          construction re-use  or recharge  directly  to  aquifers. The  Contractor’s\
      \ recharge\\n         proposals must be sufficient for recharging of the quantity of water remaining after\\\
      n         deduction of water re-used for construction. During dewatering, the contractor shall\\n        \
      \ monitor ground water levels from wells to ensure that draw down levels do not exceed\\n          allowable\
      \ limits.  The Contractor will not be permitted to directly discharge, to the\\n         drainage system,\
      \ unused ground  water  obtaining  from  the  excavation  without\\n          obtaining approval of Employer\
      \ or the Agency controlling the system.\\n\\n\\n\\n\\n\\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.           \
      \   September 2026     Section Page 72 of 114\",\n  \"text_blocks\": [\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n       \
      \ 47.039981842041016,\n        15.768376350402832,\n        561.0993041992188,\n        24.210372924804688\n\
      \      ],\n      \"block_number\": 0,\n      \"text\": \"                                                \
      \                                                                                   Safety Health & Environment\
      \ (SHE) Manual \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        26.57955551147461,\n\
      \        85.20714569091797,\n        35.93199157714844\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 1,\n      \"text\"\
      : \" \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        809.042724609375,\n     \

      Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.                              September 2026         Section Page 72\
      \ of 114 \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.4399642944336,\n        38.09945297241211,\n\

      : \"                Then the Engineer may require the Contractor to increase the frequency of impact \\n\"\
      \n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        127.44026947021484,\n        49.61947250366211,\n        512.7677001953125,\n\
      \        70.49192810058594\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 4,\n      \"text\": \"monitoring at any one\
      \ or more of the monitoring stations until the results indicate an \\nimproving and acceptable level of air\
      \ quality. \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.44023132324219,\n        83.17229461669922,\n\
      \        512.7672729492188,\n        140.4003143310547\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 5,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"47.4.10 \\nThe Contractor shall keep records of air quality monitoring (including location, date, \\ntime).\
      \ The Contractor shall submit a copy of monitoring results to the Engineer. The \\nresults should represent\
      \ a statistical evaluation of data by calculating maximum, \\nminimum, mean, standard deviation, geometric\
      \ mean and percentile calculations for \\nevaluation of frequency distribution, trends, and comparison with\
      \ emission standards. \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        82.43743133544922,\n        154.13949584960938,\n\
      \        512.7681884765625,\n        785.4517822265625\n      ],\n      \"block_number\": 6,\n      \"text\"\
      : \"47.4.11 \\nThe National Ambient Air Quality Standards given in Air (Prevention and Control of \\nPollution)\
      \ Act, 1981 may be referred by the Contractor for Limit Levels of SPM in \\nambient air which may be followed\
      \ in estimating the pollution level caused by \\nContractor’s activities.   \\n \\n47.4.12 \\nWhere the Engineer\
      \ determines that the recorded dust (TSP) level is \\nsignificantly greater than the Limit levels, the Engineer\
      \ may direct the \\nContractor to take effective remedial measures including, but not limited to, \\nreviewing\
      \ dust sources and modifying working procedures. \\n \\n47.4.13   Where the recorded baseline levels exceed\
      \ the ambient air quality standards, then at \\nsuch locations the action level is the recorded base line.\
      \ Contractor shall take all \\neffective remedial measures to contain the levels to their baseline value as\
      \ a result of \\nhis activities. The action level may be varied by and at the sole discretion of the \\nEngineer.\
      \  \\n \\n47.4.14 The Contractor shall inform the Engineer of all steps taken to investigate cause of \\nexceedance\
      \ and immediate action taken to avoid further exceedance through written \\nreports and proposals for action\
      \ under an Event Contingency Plan. \\n \\n \\n48.0 \\nWater Quality \\n \\n48.1  \\nThe Contractor shall comply\
      \ with the Indian Government legislation and other State \\nregulations in existence in Delhi insofar as they\
      \ relate to water pollution control and \\nmonitoring. A drainage system should be constructed at the commencement\
      \ of the \\nWorks, to drain off all surface water from the work site into suitable drain outlet. \\n \\n48.2\
      \  \\nThe Contractor shall provide adequate precautions to ensure that no spoil or debris of \\nany kind is\
      \ pushed, washed, falls or deposited on land adjacent to the site perimeter \\nincluding public roads or existing\
      \ stream courses and drains within or adjacent to the \\nsite. In the event of any spoil or debris from construction\
      \ works being deposited or any \\nsilt washed down to any area, then all such spoil, debris or material and\
      \ silt shall be \\nimmediately removed and the affected land and areas restored to their natural state by\
      \ \\nthe Contractor to the satisfaction of the Employer. \\n \\n48.3  \\nDue to lowering of potable water\
      \ supplies in Bangalore and subsequent contamination \\nof ground water, the Contractor is not allowed to\
      \ discharge water from the site without \\nthe approval of the Employer.  The Contractor must comply with\
      \ the requirements of \\nthe Local Ground Water Board for discharge of water arising from dewatering. Any\
      \ \\nwater obtained from dewatering systems installed in the works must be either re-used \\nfor construction\
      \ purposes and this water may subsequently be discharged to the \\ndrainage system or, if not re-used, recharged\
      \ to the ground water at suitable aquifer \\nlevels.  The Contractor must submit his proposals for approval\
      \ of Employer, on his \\nproposed locations of dewatering of excavation and collection of water for either\
      \ \\nconstruction re-use or recharge directly to aquifers. The Contractor’s recharge \\nproposals must be\
      \ sufficient for recharging of the quantity of water remaining after \\ndeduction of water re-used for construction.\
      \ During dewatering, the contractor shall \\nmonitor ground water levels from wells to ensure that draw down\
      \ levels do not exceed \\nallowable limits.  The Contractor will not be permitted to directly discharge, to\
      \ the \\ndrainage system, unused ground water obtaining from the excavation without \\nobtaining approval\
      \ of Employer or the Agency controlling the system. \\n \\n\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"bbox\": [\n        546.0439453125,\n\
      \        24.479991912841797,\n        570.0199584960938,\n        41.38799285888672\n      ],\n      \"block_number\"\

- kind: summary
  text: Volume 3 Part IV of the SHE Manual mandates specific contractor environmental management protocols, including
    restrictions on tree felling, utilization of fly ash, institution of a Waste Management Programme (WMP), hazardous
    waste handling under statutory rules, and energy conservation measures.
  evidence_ids:

  - J195a8239e4bc9422
