### J8fb9b8a53b77aca2

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4#Clause 50.0 — Landscape and Greenery|Clause 50.0 — Landscape and Greenery]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "50.0",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 77,
>       "text": "50.0    Landscape and Greenery\n\n\n          50.1    As far as is reasonably practicable, the Contractor shall maintain ecological balance\n                 by preventing deforestation and defacing of natural landscape. In respect of ecological\n                   balance, the Contractor shall observe the following instructions.\n\n\n          50.2    The Contractor  shall, so conduct his construction operations, as to prevent any\n                   avoidable destruction, scarring or defacing of natural surrounding in the vicinity of\n                   work.\n\n\n          50.3    Where destruction, scarring, damage or defacing may occur as a result of operations\n                      relating to Permanent or Temporary works, the same shall be repaired, replanted or\n                   otherwise corrected at Contractor’s expense. All work areas shall be smoothened and\n                  graded in a manner to conform to natural appearance of the landscape as directed by\n                    the Employer."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 78,
>       "text": "50.4    A suggested list of trees  / shrubs suitable for planting and landscaping is found in\n         Employer’s Project SHE Manual."
>     }

>     {
>       "pdf_page": 78,
>       "text": "51.0      Felling of Trees\n\n\n51.1    The contractor shall identify the number and type of trees that are require to be felled\n         as a result of construction of works and facilities related to Bangalore Metro Project\n        and inform the Employer.\n\n\n51.2       All trees and shrubbery, which are not specifically require to be cleared or removed for\n          construction purposes, shall be preserved and shall be protected from any damage\n           that may be caused by Contractor’s construction operations and equipment. The\n          contractor shall not fell, remove or dispose of any tree or forest produce in any land\n        handed over to him for the construction of works and facilities related to Bangalore\n         Metro except with the previous permission obtained from the Forest Department.\n\n\n51.3    The Employer shall arrange permission from the forest department for trees to be\n           felled or transplanted. The Employer will permit the removal of trees or shrubs only\n           after prior approval.\n\n51.4     Special care shall be exercised where trees or shrubs are exposed to injuries by\n          construction equipment, blasting, excavating, dumping, chemical damage or other\n          operation and the Contractor shall adequately protect such trees by used of protective\n           barriers or other methods approved by the Employer. Trees shall not be used for\n         anchorage."
>     }
>   ],

### J347d07fa7ae4694d

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4#Clause 48.0 — Water Quality|Clause 48.0 — Water Quality]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "48.0",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 76,
>       "text": "48.0     Water Quality\n\n\n48.1    The Contractor shall comply with the Indian Government legislation and other State\n          regulations in existence in Delhi insofar as they relate to water pollution control and\n          monitoring. A drainage system should be constructed at the commencement of the\n         Works, to drain off all surface water from the work site into suitable drain outlet.\n\n\n48.2    The Contractor shall provide adequate precautions to ensure that no spoil or debris of\n        any kind is pushed, washed, falls or deposited on land adjacent to the site perimeter\n          including public roads or existing stream courses and drains within or adjacent to the\n            site. In the event of any spoil or debris from construction works being deposited or any\n               silt washed down to any area, then all such spoil, debris or material and silt shall be\n         immediately removed and the affected land and areas restored to their natural state by\n          the Contractor to the satisfaction of the Employer.\n\n\n48.3    Due to lowering of potable water supplies in Bangalore and subsequent contamination\n           of ground water, the Contractor is not allowed to discharge water from the site without\n          the approval of the Employer. The Contractor must comply with the requirements of\n          the Local Ground Water Board for discharge of water arising from dewatering. Any\n         water obtained from dewatering systems installed in the works must be either re-used\n            for construction purposes and this water may subsequently be discharged to the\n         drainage system or,  if not re-used, recharged to the ground water at suitable aquifer\n           levels. The Contractor must submit his proposals for approval of Employer, on his\n         proposed locations of dewatering of excavation and collection of water for either\n          construction re-use  or recharge  directly  to  aquifers. The  Contractor’s recharge\n         proposals must be sufficient for recharging of the quantity of water remaining after\n         deduction of water re-used for construction. During dewatering, the contractor shall\n         monitor ground water levels from wells to ensure that draw down levels do not exceed\n          allowable limits.  The Contractor will not be permitted to directly discharge, to the\n         drainage system, unused ground  water  obtaining  from  the  excavation  without\n          obtaining approval of Employer or the Agency controlling the system."
>     },
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 77,
>       "text": "48.4    The Contractor shall ensure that earth, bentonite, chemicals and concrete agitator\n                  washings etc. are not deposited in the watercourses but are suitably collected and\n                   residue disposed off in a manner approved by local authorities.\n\n\n          48.5       All water and waste products (surface runoff and wastewater) arising on the site shall\n                be  collected and removed from  the  site  via a  suitable and properly designed\n                  temporary drainage system and disposed off at a location and in a manner that will\n                 cause neither pollution nor nuisance.\n\n\n          48.6    Any mud slurry from drilling, tunnelling, diaphragm wall construction or grouting etc.\n                      shall not be discharged into the drainage system unless treatment is carried out that\n                          will remove silt, mud particles, bentonite etc. The Contractor shall provide treatment\n                          facilities as necessary to prevent the discharge of contaminated ground water.\n\n\n          48.7    The Contractor shall discharge wastewater arising out of site office, canteen or toilet\n                          facilities constructed by him into sewers after obtaining prior approval of agency\n                      controlling the system.  A wastewater drainage system shall be provided to drain\n                  wastewater into the sewerage system.\n\n\n          48.8    The bentonite mixing, treatment and handling system shall be established by the\n                     contractor giving due regard to its environmental impacts. The disposal of redundant\n                    bentonite shall be carefully considered whether in bulk or liquid form. The disposal\n                     location will be advised and agreed with the relevant authorities.\n\n\n          48.9    The Contractor shall take measures to prevent discharge of oil and grease during\n                     spillage from reaching drainage system or any water body. Oil removal / interceptors\n                      shall be provided to treat oil waste from workshop areas etc.\n\n         48.10   The Contractor shall apply to the appropriate authority for installing bore wells for\n                   water supply at site."
>     }
>   ],

### J83d329b60a4b1d41

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4#Clause 55.0 — Energy Management|Clause 55.0 — Energy Management]]

> {
>   "clause_id": "55.0",
>   "fragments": [
>     {
>       "pdf_page": 79,
>       "text": "55.0     Energy Management\n\n          55.1    The contractor shall use and maintain equipment so as to conserve energy and shall\n                be able to produce demonstrable evidence of the same upon Employer’s request.\n\n\n          55.2     Measures to conserve energy include but not limited to the following:\n\n                                 i)    Use of energy efficient motors and pumps\n\n                                    ii)   Use of energy efficient lighting, which uses energy efficient luminaries\n\n                                     iii)   Adequate and uniform illumination level at construction sites suitable for the task\n\n                          iv)   Proper size and length of cables and wires to match the rating of equipment\n\n                      v)   Use of energy efficient air conditioners\n\n\n          55.3   The contractor shall design site offices maximum daylight and minimum heat gain. The\n               rooms shall be well insulated to enhance the efficiency of air conditioners and the use of\n                   solar films on windows may be used where feasible."
>     }
>   ],

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n257\n                           PART- IV ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT\n\n             47.0 ENVIORNMENTAL MANAGEMENT\n\n             47.1 ENVIRONMENTAL MONITORING\n\n              47.1.1 The  Contractor’s  Environmental  Team  shall  carry  out  the  monitoring  of\n                     environmental impacts during construction. Representative sensitive receivers in the\n                           vicinity of the works shall be monitored for noise and air quality impacts.\n              47.1.2  For carrying out impact monitoring for noise and air, equipment shall be provided,\n                    operated and maintained by the Contractor. The equipment shall be kept in a good\n                       state  of  repair  in accordance  with  the  manufacturer’s recommendations and\n                    maintained in proper working order with sufficient spare equipment available in the\n                     event of breakdown to maintain the planned monitoring program.\n              47.1.2 The calibration of monitoring instruments and their respective calibrators shall be\n                       carried out in accordance with the manufacturer’s requirements to ensure they\n                    perform to the same level of accuracy as stated in the manufacturer’s specifications.\n              47.1.3 Suspended Particulate Matter (SPM) levels shall be measured by following the\n                     standard high volume sampling method as set out in High Volume Method for\n                  Suspended Particulate, BIS: 5182-1981\n              47.1.4  24-hour average SPM concentration shall be measured by drawing air through a\n                    High Volume Sampler (HVS) fitted with pre-weighted Glass Fiber filter paper at an\n                   average flow rate not less than 1.1m3 per minute.\n              47.1.5 The minimum requirements to the specifications of sound level meter    should be\n                   as given in IS: 9779-1981\n              47.1.6  Engineer will undertake baseline monitoring to establish background levels. Action\n                      Level of the Contractor shall be based on the results of baseline monitoring\n                    program, which will be made available to him prior to start of construction.\n               47.1.7 The Contractor’s monitoring program is summarized in Table –1.\n\n                                       TABLE-1\n\n                SUMMARY OF CONTRACTOR’S MONITORING PROGRAMME\n                Parameter                   Noise                              Air\n\n          Sampling                Day Time (6.00 to 22.00 hrs):   RSPM, SPM\n                                             Lmax, Leq, L10, L90, L50            24-hours of the day\n                                        Night Time (9 PM – 6AM):      CO: 12 hrs from 8.00 to 20.00\n                                             Lmax, Leq, L10, L90, L50, Ldn          hrs.\n\n\n          Frequency               Once a week (when noise     Two 24-hour Samples every\n           At each location              generating activities are           fifteen days at uniform intervals.\n                                      underway).\n           Locations                To  be  determined  by  the  To be determined by the\n                                        Contractor  based  on  noise  Contractor based on air sensitive\n                                           sensitive receptors.              receptors.\n         Number of Locations        4 Locations                  2 Locations\n           Duration  of  Monitoring by  During Civil Construction        During Civil Construction\n           Contractor\n            Additional Requirements     Adhoc monitoring as required.   Ad hoc monitoring as required\n\n\n        47.2EVENT CONTINGENCY PLAN\n        The Contractor shall prepare an Event Contingency Plan under his Site Environmental Plan. The\n         purpose is to provide, in addition to monitoring activities, procedures for ensuring that  if any\n          environmental  exceedance  of  limiting  values  (either  accidental  or  through  inadequate\n          implementation of mitigation measures on part of the Contractor) does occur, the cause is\n          quickly identified and remedied, and that the risk of a similar event recurring is reduced.\n\n\n          47.3      Air Quality\n\n\n          47.3.1   The  Contractor  shall  take  all necessary  precautions  to minimise  fugitive  dust\n                  emissions from operations involving excavation, grading, and clearing of land and\n                    disposal of waste. He shall not allow emissions of fugitive dust from any transport,\n                    handling, construction or storage activity to remain visible in atmosphere beyond the\n                    property line of emission source for any prolonged period of time without notification to\n                    the Employer.\n\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd. ### J5051b0054febf5a3

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management]]

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n                                                          258\n47.3.2   The Contractor shall use construction equipment designed and equipped to minimise\n          or control air pollution. He shall maintain evidence of such design and equipment and\n       make these available for inspection by Employer.\n\n\n47.3.3      If  after  commencement  of  construction  activity,  Employer  believes  that  the\n          Contractor’s equipment or methods of working are causing unacceptable air pollution\n         impacts then these shall be inspected and remedial proposals shall be drawn up by\n          the Contractor, submitted for review to the Employer and implemented.\n\n\n47.3.4    In developing these remedial measures, the Contractor shall inspect and review all\n          dust sources that may be contributing to air pollution. Remedial measures include use\n           of additional / alternative equipment by the Contractor or maintenance / modification of\n           existing equipment of the Contractor.\n\n           In the event that approved remedial measures are not being implemented and serious\n         impacts persist, the Employer may direct the Contractor to suspend work until the\n        measures are implemented, as required under the Contract.\n\n\n47.3.5    Contractor’s  transport  vehicles and  other equipment  shall conform  to emission\n         standards  fixed by  Statutory  Agencies  of Government  of  India  or  the  State\n        Government from time to time. The Contractor shall carry out periodical checks and\n         undertake remedial measures including replacement,  if required, so as to operate\n           within permissible norms.\n\n\n47.3.6   The Contractor shall establish and maintain records of routine maintenance program\n            for internal combustion engine powered vehicles and equipment used on this project.\n       He shall keep records available for inspection by Employer.\n\n\n47.3.7   The Contractor shall cover loads of dust generating materials like debris and soil being\n          transported from construction  sites.  All trucks carrying loose material should be\n         covered and loaded with sufficient free- board to avoid spills through the tailboard or\n          sideboards.\n\n\n47.3.8   The Contractor shall promptly transport all excavation disposal materials of whatever\n          kind so as not to delay work on the project. Stockpiling of materials  will only be\n         allowed at sites designated by the Employer. The Contractor shall place excavation\n          materials in the dumping/disposal areas designated in the plans as given in the\n           specifications.\n\n\n47.3.9   The temporary dumping areas shall be maintained by the Contractor at all times until\n          the excavate is re-utilised for backfilling or as directed by Employer. Dust control\n            activities shall continue even during any work stoppage.\n\n\n\n\n47.3.10  The Contractor shall place material in a manner that will minimize dust production.\n          Material shall be minimized each day and wetted, to minimize dust production. During\n          dry weather, dust control methods must be used daily especially on windy, dry days to\n         prevent any dust from blowing across the site perimeter.\n\n\n47.3.11  The Contractor shall water down construction sites as required to suppress dust,\n          during handling of excavation soil or debris or during demolition. The Contractor will\n       make water sprinklers, water supply and water delivering equipment available at any\n         time that it is required for dust control use. Dust screens will be used, as feasible when\n           additional dust  control measures are needed  specially where the work  is near\n          sensitive receptors.\n\n\n47.3.12  The Contractor shall provide a wash pit or a wheel washing and/or vehicle cleaning\n             facility at the exits from work sites such as construction depots and batching plants. At\n         such facility, high-pressure water jets will be directed at the wheels of vehicles to\n        remove all spoil and dirt.\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 70 of 114",

>       "text": "47.3.2 \nThe Contractor shall use construction equipment designed and equipped to minimise \nor control air pollution. He shall maintain evidence of such design and equipment and \nmake these available for inspection by Employer. \n \n47.3.3 \nIf after commencement of construction activity, Employer believes that the \nContractor’s equipment or methods of working are causing unacceptable air pollution \nimpacts then these shall be inspected and remedial proposals shall be drawn up by \nthe Contractor, submitted for review to the Employer and implemented.  \n \n47.3.4 \nIn developing these remedial measures, the Contractor shall inspect and review all \ndust sources that may be contributing to air pollution. Remedial measures include use \nof additional / alternative equipment by the Contractor or maintenance / modification of \nexisting equipment of the Contractor. \nIn the event that approved remedial measures are not being implemented and serious \nimpacts persist, the Employer may direct the Contractor to suspend work until the \nmeasures are implemented, as required under the Contract. \n \n47.3.5 \nContractor’s transport vehicles and other equipment shall conform to emission \nstandards fixed by Statutory Agencies of Government of India or the State \nGovernment from time to time.  The Contractor shall carry out periodical checks and \nundertake remedial measures including replacement, if required, so as to operate \nwithin permissible norms.   \n \n47.3.6 \nThe Contractor shall establish and maintain records of routine maintenance program \nfor internal combustion engine powered vehicles and equipment used on this project.  \nHe shall keep records available for inspection by Employer. \n \n47.3.7 \nThe Contractor shall cover loads of dust generating materials like debris and soil being \ntransported from construction sites. All trucks carrying loose material should be \ncovered and loaded with sufficient free- board to avoid spills through the tailboard or \nsideboards. \n \n47.3.8 \nThe Contractor shall promptly transport all excavation disposal materials of whatever \nkind so as not to delay work on the project. Stockpiling of materials will only be \nallowed at sites designated by the Employer. The Contractor shall place excavation \nmaterials in the dumping/disposal areas designated in the plans as given in the \nspecifications.  \n \n47.3.9  \nThe temporary dumping areas shall be maintained by the Contractor at all times until \nthe excavate is re-utilised for backfilling or as directed by Employer. Dust control \nactivities shall continue even during any work stoppage. \n \n \n47.3.10 \nThe Contractor shall place material in a manner that will minimize dust production.  \nMaterial shall be minimized each day and wetted, to minimize dust production.  During \ndry weather, dust control methods must be used daily especially on windy, dry days to \nprevent any dust from blowing across the site perimeter. \n \n47.3.11 \nThe Contractor shall water down construction sites as required to suppress dust, \nduring handling of excavation soil or debris or during demolition. The Contractor will \nmake water sprinklers, water supply and water delivering equipment available at any \ntime that it is required for dust control use. Dust screens will be used, as feasible when \nadditional dust control measures are needed specially where the work is near \nsensitive receptors. \n \n47.3.12 \nThe Contractor shall provide a wash pit or a wheel washing and/or vehicle cleaning \nfacility at the exits from work sites such as construction depots and batching plants. At \nsuch facility, high-pressure water jets will be directed at the wheels of vehicles to \nremove all spoil and dirt.  \n"

### Jbf778e79ee5d7dc3

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management]]

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n259\n\n          47.3.13  The Contractor shall design and implement his blasting techniques so as to minimise\n                     dust, noise, vibration generation and prevention fly rock.\n\n\n          47.3.14   Blasting technique should be consistent not only with nature and quaintly of rock to be\n                    blasted but also the location of blasting.\n\n          47.3.15  The  contractor  shall  give  preference  to  explosives  with  better  environmental\n                      characteristics.\n\n          47.3.16  The Contractor shall protect structures, utilities, pavements roads and other facilities\n                  from disfiguration and damage as a result of his activities. Where this is not possible,\n                    the contractor  shall restore the structures,  utilities, pavements, roads and other\n                          facilities to their original or better, failing which the rectification/restoration work shall\n                be carried out at the risk and cost of the contractor.\n\n\n          47.3.17  The Contractor shall submit to the Employer an Air Monitoring and Control Plan\n               (AMCP) under contract specific Site Environmental Plan to guide construction activity\n                     insofar as it relates to monitoring, controlling and mitigating air pollution.\n\n\n          47.4    AIR MONITORING\n          47.4.1    Construction activities that will generate dust impacts include excavation (including\n                     related activities), material handling and stockpiling, vehicular movement, and wind\n                   erosion of unpaved work areas.\n\n          47.4.2 The impact of fugitive dust on ambient air pollution depends on the quantity generated, as\n                     well as the drift potential of the dust particles injected into the atmosphere. Large dust\n                      particles will settle out near the source and smaller particles are likely to undergo\n                    dispersal over greater distance from the sources and impeded settling. SPM levels will\n                be monitored to evaluate the dust impact during the construction phase of the Project.\n\n          47.4.3   The  Air  Quality  Monitoring and  Control Plan (AMCP)  in  contract-specific  Site\n                   Environmental Plan prepared by the Contractor shall establish procedures to monitor\n                  impact air quality and measures to control air pollution including dust suppression due\n                      to construction activities at work sites. This plan shall contain description of activities\n                      that  will cause degradation  in  air  quality, environmental procedures  to manage\n                     pollutants to minimise the  air  pollution, monitoring program, record keeping and\n                      reporting.\n\n          47.4.4   The Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks specified, and will inspect\n                  necessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of air quality given in\n               AMCP.\n          47.4.5    Information gathered during the AMCP  will be catalogued and maintained by the\n                    Contractor and shall be available for review by the Engineer.\n\n          47.4.6   The exact location of the air monitoring stations located near air sensitive receptors\n                    adjoining the construction sites, such as residences, schools, hotels and hospitals and\n                  placement of monitoring equipment thereat shall be agreed with the Engineer prior to\n               commencement of air monitoring program.\n\n          47.4.7   Impact monitoring during the course  of the Works  shall be carried out  at the\n                    monitoring stations for two days (continuous twenty-four hours) every fifteen days and\n                 where there is a perceived air quality problem.\n          47.4.8   The Contractor shall construct suitable fence, lockable gate, 220V AC power point and\n                     suitable access at each air monitoring station.  Monitoring stations shall be free from\n                      local obstructions or sheltering.\n\n\n          47.4.9   Should impact monitoring record dust levels which are:\n\n\n                       indicative of a deteriorating situation such that closer monitoring is reasonably\n                          indicated, or\n\n\n                      when in the opinion of the Engineer additional measurements are required in view\n                          of deteriorating air quality,\n\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 71 of 114",

>       "text": " \n47.3.13 \nThe Contractor shall design and implement his blasting techniques so as to minimise \ndust, noise, vibration generation and prevention fly rock. \n \n47.3.14 \nBlasting technique should be consistent not only with nature and quaintly of rock to be \nblasted but also the location of blasting. \n47.3.15 \nThe contractor shall give preference to explosives with better environmental \ncharacteristics. \n47.3.16 \nThe Contractor shall protect structures, utilities, pavements roads and other facilities \nfrom disfiguration and damage as a result of his activities. Where this is not possible, \nthe contractor shall restore the structures, utilities, pavements, roads and other \nfacilities to their original or better, failing which the rectification/restoration work shall \nbe carried out at the risk and cost of the contractor.    \n \n47.3.17 \nThe Contractor shall submit to the Employer an Air Monitoring and Control Plan \n(AMCP) under contract specific Site Environmental Plan to guide construction activity \ninsofar as it relates to monitoring, controlling and mitigating air pollution. \n \n47.4         AIR MONITORING \n47.4.1 \nConstruction activities that will generate dust impacts include excavation (including \nrelated activities), material handling and stockpiling, vehicular movement, and wind \nerosion of unpaved work areas.  \n \n47.4.2 The impact of fugitive dust on ambient air pollution depends on the quantity generated, as \nwell as the drift potential of the dust particles injected into the atmosphere.  Large dust \nparticles will settle out near the source and smaller particles are likely to undergo \ndispersal over greater distance from the sources and impeded settling. SPM levels will \nbe monitored to evaluate the dust impact during the construction phase of the Project. \n \n47.4.3 \nThe Air Quality Monitoring and Control Plan (AMCP) in contract-specific Site \nEnvironmental Plan prepared by the Contractor shall establish procedures to monitor \nimpact air quality and measures to control air pollution including dust suppression due \nto construction activities at work sites. This plan shall contain description of activities \nthat will cause degradation in air quality, environmental procedures to manage \npollutants to minimise the air pollution, monitoring program, record keeping and \nreporting.   \n \n47.4.4 \nThe Engineer shall monitor Contractor’s performance of tasks specified, and will inspect \nnecessary records, reports and procedures related to the control of air quality given in \nAMCP. \n47.4.5 \nInformation gathered during the AMCP will be catalogued and maintained by the \nContractor and shall be available for review by the Engineer. \n \n47.4.6 \nThe exact location of the air monitoring stations located near air sensitive receptors \nadjoining the construction sites, such as residences, schools, hotels and hospitals and \nplacement of monitoring equipment thereat shall be agreed with the Engineer prior to \ncommencement of air monitoring program.  \n \n47.4.7 \nImpact monitoring during the course of the Works shall be carried out at the \nmonitoring stations for two days (continuous twenty-four hours) every fifteen days and \nwhere there is a perceived air quality problem.   \n47.4.8 \nThe Contractor shall construct suitable fence, lockable gate, 220V AC power point and \nsuitable access at each air monitoring station.  Monitoring stations shall be free from \nlocal obstructions or sheltering. \n"

### Ja56bcc2428ec77d1

>   "rotation": 0,
>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n261\n          48.4    The Contractor shall ensure that earth, bentonite, chemicals and concrete agitator\n                  washings etc. are not deposited in the watercourses but are suitably collected and\n                   residue disposed off in a manner approved by local authorities.\n\n\n          48.5       All water and waste products (surface runoff and wastewater) arising on the site shall\n                be  collected and removed from  the  site  via a  suitable and properly designed\n                  temporary drainage system and disposed off at a location and in a manner that will\n                 cause neither pollution nor nuisance.\n\n\n          48.6    Any mud slurry from drilling, tunnelling, diaphragm wall construction or grouting etc.\n                      shall not be discharged into the drainage system unless treatment is carried out that\n                          will remove silt, mud particles, bentonite etc. The Contractor shall provide treatment\n                          facilities as necessary to prevent the discharge of contaminated ground water.\n\n\n          48.7    The Contractor shall discharge wastewater arising out of site office, canteen or toilet\n                          facilities constructed by him into sewers after obtaining prior approval of agency\n                      controlling the system.  A wastewater drainage system shall be provided to drain\n                  wastewater into the sewerage system.\n\n\n          48.8    The bentonite mixing, treatment and handling system shall be established by the\n                     contractor giving due regard to its environmental impacts. The disposal of redundant\n                    bentonite shall be carefully considered whether in bulk or liquid form. The disposal\n                     location will be advised and agreed with the relevant authorities.\n\n\n          48.9    The Contractor shall take measures to prevent discharge of oil and grease during\n                     spillage from reaching drainage system or any water body. Oil removal / interceptors\n                      shall be provided to treat oil waste from workshop areas etc.\n\n         48.10   The Contractor shall apply to the appropriate authority for installing bore wells for\n                   water supply at site.\n\n\n          49.0     Archaeological and Historical Preservation\n\n          49.1    The contractor shall seek to accommodate archaeological and historical preservation\n                  concerns that may arise due to the construction of the project especially in close\n                        vicinity of such areas where such monuments may be located.\n\n\n          49.2    The contractor shall consult the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI) and other\n                      parties, on the advise of the Employer, to identify and assess construction effects and\n                 seek ways to avoid, minimize or mitigate adverse effects on such monuments.\n\n\n          49.3     Adverse  effects may  include  reasonably  foreseeable  effects  caused  by  the\n                    construction that may occur later in time, be farther removed in distance or those that\n                         alter, howsoever temporarily, the significance of the structure.\n\n\n          50.0    Landscape and Greenery\n\n\n          50.1    As far as is reasonably practicable, the Contractor shall maintain ecological balance\n                 by preventing deforestation and defacing of natural landscape. In respect of ecological\n                   balance, the Contractor shall observe the following instructions.\n\n\n          50.2    The Contractor  shall, so conduct his construction operations, as to prevent any\n                   avoidable destruction, scarring or defacing of natural surrounding in the vicinity of\n                   work.\n\n\n          50.3    Where destruction, scarring, damage or defacing may occur as a result of operations\n                      relating to Permanent or Temporary works, the same shall be repaired, replanted or\n                   otherwise corrected at Contractor’s expense. All work areas shall be smoothened and\n                  graded in a manner to conform to natural appearance of the landscape as directed by\n                    the Employer.\n\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 73 of 114",

>       "text": "48.4  \nThe Contractor shall ensure that earth, bentonite, chemicals and concrete agitator \nwashings etc. are not deposited in the watercourses but are suitably collected and \nresidue disposed off in a manner approved by local authorities.  \n \n48.5  \nAll water and waste products (surface runoff and wastewater) arising on the site shall \nbe collected and removed from the site via a suitable and properly designed \ntemporary drainage system and disposed off at a location and in a manner that will \ncause neither pollution nor nuisance.  \n \n48.6  \nAny mud slurry from drilling, tunnelling, diaphragm wall construction or grouting etc. \nshall not be discharged into the drainage system unless treatment is carried out that \nwill remove silt, mud particles, bentonite etc. The Contractor shall provide treatment \nfacilities as necessary to prevent the discharge of contaminated ground water. \n \n48.7  \nThe Contractor shall discharge wastewater arising out of site office, canteen or toilet \nfacilities constructed by him into sewers after obtaining prior approval of agency \ncontrolling the system.  A wastewater drainage system shall be provided to drain \nwastewater into the sewerage system.   \n \n48.8  \nThe bentonite mixing, treatment and handling system shall be established by the \ncontractor giving due regard to its environmental impacts. The disposal of redundant \nbentonite shall be carefully considered whether in bulk or liquid form. The disposal \nlocation will be advised and agreed with the relevant authorities. \n \n48.9  \nThe Contractor shall take measures to prevent discharge of oil and grease during \nspillage from reaching drainage system or any water body. Oil removal / interceptors \nshall be provided to treat oil waste from workshop areas etc. \n48.10 \nThe Contractor shall apply to the appropriate authority for installing bore wells for \nwater supply at site. \n \n49.0  \nArchaeological and Historical Preservation \n49.1  \nThe contractor shall seek to accommodate archaeological and historical preservation \nconcerns that may arise due to the construction of the project especially in close \nvicinity of such areas where such monuments may be located.  \n \n49.2  \nThe contractor shall consult the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI) and other \nparties, on the advise of the Employer, to identify and assess construction effects and \nseek ways to avoid, minimize or mitigate adverse effects on such monuments. \n \n49.3   \nAdverse effects may include reasonably foreseeable effects caused by the \nconstruction that may occur later in time, be farther removed in distance or those that \nalter, howsoever temporarily, the significance of the structure.   \n \n50.0 \nLandscape and Greenery \n \n50.1  \nAs far as is reasonably practicable, the Contractor shall maintain ecological balance \nby preventing deforestation and defacing of natural landscape. In respect of ecological \nbalance, the Contractor shall observe the following instructions. \n \n50.2 \nThe Contractor shall, so conduct his construction operations, as to prevent any \navoidable destruction, scarring or defacing of natural surrounding in the vicinity of \nwork. \n \n50.3   \nWhere destruction, scarring, damage or defacing may occur as a result of operations \nrelating to Permanent or Temporary works, the same shall be repaired, replanted or \notherwise corrected at Contractor’s expense. All work areas shall be smoothened and \ngraded in a manner to conform to natural appearance of the landscape as directed by \nthe Employer.  \n"

### J493f5af431b2b948

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management]]

>   "rotation": 0,
>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n                                                          260\n        Then the Engineer may require the Contractor to increase the frequency of impact\n          monitoring at any one or more of the monitoring stations until the results indicate an\n         improving and acceptable level of air quality.\n\n\n47.4.10  The Contractor shall keep records of air quality monitoring (including location, date,\n           time). The Contractor shall submit a copy of monitoring results to the Engineer. The\n           results should represent a  statistical evaluation of data by calculating maximum,\n        minimum, mean, standard deviation, geometric mean and percentile calculations for\n          evaluation of frequency distribution, trends, and comparison with emission standards.\n\n\n47.4.11  The National Ambient Air Quality Standards given in Air (Prevention and Control of\n           Pollution) Act, 1981 may be referred by the Contractor for Limit Levels of SPM in\n         ambient  air which may be followed  in estimating the  pollution  level caused by\n          Contractor’s activities.\n\n47.4.12  Where  the  Engineer  determines  that  the  recorded  dust  (TSP)  level  is\n          significantly  greater  than  the  Limit  levels,  the Engineer may  direct  the\n         Contractor to take effective remedial measures including, but not limited to,\n         reviewing dust sources and modifying working procedures.\n\n47.4.13  Where the recorded baseline levels exceed the ambient air quality standards, then at\n         such locations the action level is the recorded base line. Contractor shall take all\n           effective remedial measures to contain the levels to their baseline value as a result of\n           his activities. The action level may be varied by and at the sole discretion of the\n         Engineer.\n\n47.4.14 The Contractor shall inform the Engineer of  all steps taken to investigate cause of\n        exceedance and immediate action taken to avoid further exceedance through written\n         reports and proposals for action under an Event Contingency Plan.\n\n\n\n48.0     Water Quality\n\n\n48.1    The Contractor shall comply with the Indian Government legislation and other State\n          regulations in existence in Delhi insofar as they relate to water pollution control and\n          monitoring. A drainage system should be constructed at the commencement of the\n         Works, to drain off all surface water from the work site into suitable drain outlet.\n\n\n48.2    The Contractor shall provide adequate precautions to ensure that no spoil or debris of\n        any kind is pushed, washed, falls or deposited on land adjacent to the site perimeter\n          including public roads or existing stream courses and drains within or adjacent to the\n            site. In the event of any spoil or debris from construction works being deposited or any\n               silt washed down to any area, then all such spoil, debris or material and silt shall be\n         immediately removed and the affected land and areas restored to their natural state by\n          the Contractor to the satisfaction of the Employer.\n\n\n48.3    Due to lowering of potable water supplies in Bangalore and subsequent contamination\n           of ground water, the Contractor is not allowed to discharge water from the site without\n          the approval of the Employer. The Contractor must comply with the requirements of\n          the Local Ground Water Board for discharge of water arising from dewatering. Any\n         water obtained from dewatering systems installed in the works must be either re-used\n            for construction purposes and this water may subsequently be discharged to the\n         drainage system or,  if not re-used, recharged to the ground water at suitable aquifer\n           levels. The Contractor must submit his proposals for approval of Employer, on his\n         proposed locations of dewatering of excavation and collection of water for either\n          construction re-use  or recharge  directly  to  aquifers. The  Contractor’s recharge\n         proposals must be sufficient for recharging of the quantity of water remaining after\n         deduction of water re-used for construction. During dewatering, the contractor shall\n         monitor ground water levels from wells to ensure that draw down levels do not exceed\n          allowable limits.  The Contractor will not be permitted to directly discharge, to the\n         drainage system, unused ground  water  obtaining  from  the  excavation  without\n          obtaining approval of Employer or the Agency controlling the system.\n\n\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 72 of 114",

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n                                                          262\n\n50.4    A suggested list of trees  / shrubs suitable for planting and landscaping is found in\n         Employer’s Project SHE Manual.\n\n\n51.0      Felling of Trees\n\n\n51.1    The contractor shall identify the number and type of trees that are require to be felled\n         as a result of construction of works and facilities related to Bangalore Metro Project\n        and inform the Employer.\n\n\n51.2       All trees and shrubbery, which are not specifically require to be cleared or removed for\n          construction purposes, shall be preserved and shall be protected from any damage\n           that may be caused by Contractor’s construction operations and equipment. The\n          contractor shall not fell, remove or dispose of any tree or forest produce in any land\n        handed over to him for the construction of works and facilities related to Bangalore\n         Metro except with the previous permission obtained from the Forest Department.\n\n\n51.3    The Employer shall arrange permission from the forest department for trees to be\n           felled or transplanted. The Employer will permit the removal of trees or shrubs only\n           after prior approval.\n\n51.4     Special care shall be exercised where trees or shrubs are exposed to injuries by\n          construction equipment, blasting, excavating, dumping, chemical damage or other\n          operation and the Contractor shall adequately protect such trees by used of protective\n           barriers or other methods approved by the Employer. Trees shall not be used for\n         anchorage.\n\n\n52.0     Fly Ash\n\n\n52.1    The Employer may require the contractor to use fly ash as a percentage substitution of\n         cement, in concrete for certain structures and works.\n\n\n52.2      In all such uses of Fly Ash, the contractor shall maintain a detailed record of usage of\n          Fly Ash. The contractor shall also collect related details and provide to the Employer.\n\n\n52.3    The reporting details on consumption of Fly Ash are found in Employer’s SHE Manual.\n\n\n53.0    Waste\n\n\n53.1    The contractor is required to develop, institute and maintain a Waste Management\n        Programme (WMP) during the construction of the project for his works, which may\n          include: -\n\n\n                i)     Identification of disposal sites.\n\n                  ii)     Identification of quantities to be excavated and disposed off.\n\n                  iii)    Identification of split between waste and inert material\n\n             iv)    Identification of amounts intended to be stored temporarily on site location of\n              such storage.\n\n           v)     Identification of intended transport means and route.\n\n             vi)   Obtaining permission, where required, for disposal.\n\n\n53.2     Such a mechanism  is intended to ensure that the designation of areas  for the\n         segregation and  temporary  storage  of  reusable and  recyclable  materials  are\n          incorporate into the WMP. The WMP should be prepared and submitted to the\n         Engineer for approval.\n\n53.3    The Contractor shall handle waste in a manner that ensures they are held securely\n          without loss or leakage thus minimizing potential for pollution. The Contractor shall\n          maintain and clean waste storage areas regularly.\n\n\n\nBangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 74 of 114",

### J195a8239e4bc9422

[[OKF_bundle/Technical/Vol3/vol3_part4#Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management|Safety, Health and Environment Manual – Part IV: Environmental Management]]

>   "text": "Safety Health & Environment (SHE) Manual\n263\n          53.4    The Contractor shall remove waste in a timely manner and disposed off at landfill sites\n                       after obtaining approval of the competent authorities namely BBMP, BDA, BMRDA,\n              BWSSB.\n\n\n          53.5     Burning of wastes is prohibited. The Contractor shall not burn debris or vegetation or\n                    construction waste on the site but remove it in accordance with 50.1 above.\n\n\n          53.6    The Contractor shall make arrangement to dispose of metal scrap and other saleable\n                 waste to authorized dealer and make available to the Employer on request, records of\n                 such sales.\n\n          54.0     Hazardous Waste Management\n\n          54.1         If encountered or generated as a result of Contractor’s activity, then waste classified\n                 as hazardous under the “Hazardous Wastes (Management & Handling) Rules, 1989,\n                amendments 2000, 2003” shall be disposed off in a manner in compliance with the\n                  procedure given in the rules under the aforesaid act.\n\n\n          54.2     Chemicals classified as hazardous chemicals under “Manufacture, Storage and Import\n                      of Hazardous Chemical Rules, 1989 of Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 shall be\n                  disposed off in a manner in compliance with the procedure given in the rules under the\n                    aforesaid act.\n\n\n          54.3    The contractor shall identify the nature and quantity of hazardous waste generated as\n                a result of his activities and shall file a ‘Request for Authorisation’ with Bangalore\n                     Pollution Control Board along with a map showing the location of storage area.\n\n\n          54.4     Outside the storage area, the contractor shall place a ‘display board’, which  will\n                    display quantity and nature of hazardous waste, on date. Hazardous Waste needs to\n                be stored in a secure place\n\n\n          54.5         It shall be the responsibility of the contractor to ensure that hazardous wastes are\n                     stored, based on the composition, in a manner suitable for handling, storage and\n                      transport. The labelling and packaging is required to be easily visible and be able to\n                   withstand physical conditions and climatic factors.\n\n\n          54.6    The contractor shall approach only Authorised Recyclers of Hazardous Waste for\n                    disposal of Hazardous Waste, under intimation to the Employer.\n\n\n          54.7      Submittal of all environment related documents and records pertaining to monitoring\n                and trend analysis on key parameters such as but not limited to consumption/efficient\n                 use of resources such as energy, water, material such as cement, fly ash, iron and\n                       steel, recycle/reuse of waste etc that shall have demonstrated continual improvement\n                        in the implementation of Environmental management System. Failure to do so the\n                  employer shall impose appropriate penalty as indicated under penalty clause.\n\n          55.0     Energy Management\n\n          55.1    The contractor shall use and maintain equipment so as to conserve energy and shall\n                be able to produce demonstrable evidence of the same upon Employer’s request.\n\n\n          55.2     Measures to conserve energy include but not limited to the following:\n\n                                 i)    Use of energy efficient motors and pumps\n\n                                    ii)   Use of energy efficient lighting, which uses energy efficient luminaries\n\n                                     iii)   Adequate and uniform illumination level at construction sites suitable for the task\n\n                          iv)   Proper size and length of cables and wires to match the rating of equipment\n\n                      v)   Use of energy efficient air conditioners\n\n\n          55.3   The contractor shall design site offices maximum daylight and minimum heat gain. The\n               rooms shall be well insulated to enhance the efficiency of air conditioners and the use of\n                   solar films on windows may be used where feasible.\n\n\n\n\n         Bangalore Metro Rail Corporation Ltd.              September 2026     Section Page 75 of 114",

>       "text": "53.4  \nThe Contractor shall remove waste in a timely manner and disposed off at landfill sites \nafter obtaining approval of the competent authorities namely BBMP, BDA, BMRDA, \nBWSSB. \n \n53.5  \nBurning of wastes is prohibited. The Contractor shall not burn debris or vegetation or \nconstruction waste on the site but remove it in accordance with 50.1 above.  \n \n53.6  \nThe Contractor shall make arrangement to dispose of metal scrap and other saleable \nwaste to authorized dealer and make available to the Employer on request, records of \nsuch sales.    \n54.0 \nHazardous Waste Management \n54.1  \nIf encountered or generated as a result of Contractor’s activity, then waste classified \nas hazardous under the “Hazardous Wastes (Management & Handling) Rules, 1989, \namendments 2000, 2003” shall be disposed off in a manner in compliance with the \nprocedure given in the rules under the aforesaid act.   \n \n54.2 \nChemicals classified as hazardous chemicals under “Manufacture, Storage and Import \nof Hazardous Chemical Rules, 1989 of Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 shall be \ndisposed off in a manner in compliance with the procedure given in the rules under the \naforesaid act.   \n \n54.3 \nThe contractor shall identify the nature and quantity of hazardous waste generated as \na result of his activities and shall file a ‘Request for Authorisation’ with Bangalore  \nPollution Control Board along with a map showing the location of storage area.  \n \n54.4 \nOutside the storage area, the contractor shall place a ‘display board’, which will \ndisplay quantity and nature of hazardous waste, on date. Hazardous Waste needs to \nbe stored in a secure place \n \n54.5 \nIt shall be the responsibility of the contractor to ensure that hazardous wastes are \nstored, based on the composition, in a manner suitable for handling, storage and \ntransport. The labelling and packaging is required to be easily visible and be able to \nwithstand physical conditions and climatic factors. \n \n54.6 \nThe contractor shall approach only Authorised Recyclers of Hazardous Waste for \ndisposal of Hazardous Waste, under intimation to the Employer. \n \n54.7 \nSubmittal of all environment related documents and records pertaining to monitoring \nand trend analysis on key parameters such as but not limited to consumption/efficient \nuse of resources such as energy, water, material such as cement, fly ash, iron and \nsteel, recycle/reuse of waste etc that shall have demonstrated continual improvement \nin the implementation of Environmental management System. Failure to do so the \nemployer shall impose appropriate penalty as indicated under penalty clause. \n55.0 \nEnergy Management \n55.1  \nThe contractor shall use and maintain equipment so as to conserve energy and shall \nbe able to produce demonstrable evidence of the same upon Employer’s request. \n \n55.2  \nMeasures to conserve energy include but not limited to the following: \ni) \nUse of energy efficient motors and pumps  \nii) \nUse of energy efficient lighting, which uses energy efficient luminaries \niii) \nAdequate and uniform illumination level at construction sites suitable for the task \niv) \nProper size and length of cables and wires to match the rating of equipment \nv) \nUse of energy efficient air conditioners \n \n55.3  \nThe contractor shall design site offices maximum daylight and minimum heat gain. The \nrooms shall be well insulated to enhance the efficiency of air conditioners and the use of \nsolar films on windows may be used where feasible.   \n"
